  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 15:19 (thoughts), Mar 7:21 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22576** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Behaviour discovered: weighing up what is said or taught. The elements show the command to the others to weigh what the prophets say (22487) and the Preacher weighing proverbs with care (22503). They belong together because in both the weighing is an evaluation of words spoken or taught. This gathers the elements named by the traced observations and states no claim about what the behaviour is.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Jos 1:8 (meditate), 1Cor 14:29 (weigh)
- **Observation 22577** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The heart or the mind is named as the place of the working in three subgroups: of the attending (the meditation of the heart, the heart of the righteous that ponders, Mary pondering in her heart), of the supposing (the heart or mind named with the thought within three words in several verses) and of the thoughts (all fifteen verses of the pairing). The heart is the place stated in each, across the elements of three subgroups.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 19:14 (meditation), Psa 49:3 (meditation)
- **Observation 22578** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Knowing stands beside the working in three subgroups: knowledge as what the supposer thinks he has or lacks (22525), the knowing of Mar 12:24 as the ground of error (22479) and the knowing of another as the way thoughts are held (22536). In the first the one who thinks is the knower, in the second the lack of knowing is the ground, and in the third another knows the thoughts.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 8:2 (imagines)
- **Observation 22579** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Fear and alarm stand with the working in three subgroups: fear within three words before the supposition (22521), the fear of God that describes the one the Lord considers (22501) and the thoughts that alarm (22537). The fear precedes the supposition, accompanies the one considered and is caused by the thoughts, according to the verses of each.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Gen 31:31 (thought), Exo 2:14 (thought)
- **Observation 22580** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The working appears in a negated form in each of five subgroups: the supposition forbidden (22511), regard withheld (22463), the setting of the mind negated (22454), the reckoning negated in 1Ki 10:21 (22490), the ground denied (22480) and thoughts not known (22542). In each the negator reaches the member in the verses named, and the denial is stated beside what is affirmed in the opposite verses.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 5:17 (think), Mat 10:34 (think)
- **Observation 22581** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): God, the Lord and Jesus stand as the one who knows, considers, regards or corrects in several subgroups: the Lord considering Job and weighing paths (22488), knowing thoughts (22536), Jesus rebuking Peter (22459), the Lord regarded or not regarded (22466) and the correction of a supposition by Jesus (22511). The divine entity is the knower, the object or the one who corrects, and the direction differs by subgroup.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 1:8 (considered)
- **Observation 22582** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Anger and the supposition meet in three verses. In Gen 18:30 and Gen 18:32 the Lord's anger is what Abraham asks not to be roused, 2 and 4 words before the supposed number, and the supposition is the case he puts while the anger is held back; in 2Ki 5:11 Naaman's own anger stands 4 words before his quoted thought. The anger is the setting of the supposition, the Lord's in the first two and the thinker's in the third.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 18:30 (suppose), Gen 18:32 (suppose), 2Ki 5:11 (thought)
- **Observation 22583** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Believing and the supposition meet in four verses with different ties. In Pro 14:15 the simple believes everything 3 words before the prudent gives thought, and the two are set against each other; in Rom 12:3, Phili 3:4 and Jam 1:26 the faith, confidence or religion is the thing the person thinks of himself, 1 to 12 words from the member. The supposition is about one's own standing in belief.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Pro 14:15 (thought), Rom 12:3 (think), Phili 3:4 (thinks), Jam 1:26 (thinks)
- **Observation 22584** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Blessing words stand far from the supposition in three verses: grace 11 words before in Rom 12:3, a willing gift 16 words after in 2Cor 9:5 and grace 16 after in Heb 10:29. At these distances the partner is in another clause, and the two work together only in that the same verse speaks of what is given and of what is thought of oneself or others; no tie of grammar joins them.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Rom 12:3 (think), 2Cor 9:5 (thought), Heb 10:29 (think)
- **Observation 22585** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Calling and the supposition meet in four verses. In 2Ki 5:11 (4 words after) and Jer 3:19 (1 after) the call is what the thinker expected would be made; in Jon 1:6 the command to call on his god stands 4 words before the hope that the god will give thought to them; in Gen 26:9 the calling of Isaac is 12 words before and is a separate act. The call is the expected or commanded act that the supposition concerns.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 26:9 (thought), 2Ki 5:11 (thought), Jer 3:19 (thought), Jon 1:6 (thought)
- **Observation 22586** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): War and the supposition meet in four verses: in 2Ki 18:20 and Isa 36:5 war stands 5 words after the thought that words are strategy and power for it, in Mic 2:8 it is 1 word after the thought of the passersby, and in 1Sa 18:17 fight and battles stand 4 and 2 words before Saul's quoted thought. Conflict is what the supposed strength or the plan is for.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:17 (thought), 2Ki 18:20 (think), Isa 36:5 (think), Mic 2:8 (thought)
- **Observation 22587** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Comfort and the supposition meet in three verses with different relations. In Psa 119:52 the thinking of the Lord's rules and the comfort taken are in one clause, comfort 4 words after; in Job 12:5 the at-ease (1 word after the thought) hold contempt for misfortune; in Joh 11:31 the consoling stands 10 words before the supposition that explains why they followed Mary out.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 12:5 (thought), Psa 119:52 (think), Joh 11:31 (supposing)
- **Observation 22588** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Commanding and the supposition meet in five verses. In Num 36:6 the Lord's commands stand 4 words before the thought of whom they think best; in 1Cor 14:37 the command (11 words after) is what the one who thinks he is a prophet is to acknowledge; in Psa 50:21 the charge (2 after) follows the false supposition about God; in Rom 12:3 and 2Sa 13:32 the partner is 8 and 12 words after, in later clauses. The supposition is set against a command or charge.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Num 36:6 (think), 2Sa 13:32 (suppose), Psa 50:21 (thought), Rom 12:3 (think), 1Cor 14:37 (thinks)
- **Observation 22589** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Concealing and the supposition meet in three verses: the covering of her face (2 words after) is the ground of Judah's supposition in Gen 38:15; hiding (2 before) and secretly (1 before) stand beside the quoted thoughts of the Syrians and of the wicked in 2Ki 7:12 and Psa 64:5. The thought is formed from, or alongside, something concealed.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 38:15 (thought), 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), Psa 64:5 (thinking)
- **Observation 22590** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Judging and the supposition meet in four verses. In Joh 5:45 (accuse, 3 after) and Rom 2:3 (judge, 3 after) the supposition concerns accusation or judgment; in Luk 7:43 the judging (8 after) is the answer given to Simon's supposition; in Exo 2:14 'judge' is 8 words before, in the question the accuser puts to Moses. The supposition and the judging are set side by side in each.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Exo 2:14 (thought), Luk 7:43 (suppose), Joh 5:45 (think), Rom 2:3 (suppose)
- **Observation 22591** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Persuading and the supposition meet in three verses: persuaded stands 8 words before the supposition of the crowds in Act 14:19 and 13 before Paul's thought of the unclean in Rom 14:14, and obeying stands 3 words after the thought taken captive in 2Cor 10:5. The persuasion is a separate earlier act in the first two, and the obedience is the end of the thought in the third.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Act 14:19 (supposing), Rom 14:14 (thinks), 2Cor 10:5 (thought)
- **Observation 22592** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Deceiving and the supposition meet in three verses: 6 words before in 1Cor 3:18, 5 after in Gal 6:3 and 8 after in Jam 1:26. In all three the person thinks something of himself and the verse names self-deception beside it, so the supposition about oneself and the deceit of oneself are stated together.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 1Cor 3:18 (thinks), Gal 6:3 (thinks), Jam 1:26 (thinks)
- **Observation 22593** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Departing and the supposition meet in four verses. In Joh 11:31 the going out is 2 words after the supposition that explains it; in 1Sa 27:11 'leave' is 5 words before the quoted thought of David; in Ecc 9:10 and Mat 18:12 the going or leaving is 7 and 12 words from the member in other clauses. Only in Joh 11:31 is the departure the act that the supposition leads to.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 1Sa 27:11 (thinking), Ecc 9:10 (thought), Mat 18:12 (think), Joh 11:31 (supposing)
- **Observation 22594** (layer-3, tag statement, marker inner_being, status draft): Desire words and the supposition meet in five verses at varying distance: 'desires' 12 words before in 1Sa 18:25, 'willing' 9 before in 1Ch 28:9, 'yearns' 5 after in Jam 4:5; the surface 'will' of 2Sa 14:17 and 2Ki 7:12 is the future particle of the quoted speech and not a desire, flagged in #2536. Desire stands in the same sentence as the thought but not within it.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:25 (thought), 2Sa 14:17 (thought), 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Jam 4:5 (suppose)
- **Observation 22595** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Destroying and the supposition meet in five verses. In Gen 18:28, 18:31 and 18:32 the destroying is what the Lord is asked not to do under each supposed number; in Isa 10:7 it is 3 words after the thought and is the purpose in the heart that is said not to be what he thinks; in 2Cor 10:5 it is 12 words before and is an act on arguments. The supposition is the case on which the destroying is argued or the plan that destroying belongs to.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Gen 18:28 (suppose), Gen 18:31 (suppose), Gen 18:32 (suppose), Isa 10:7 (think), 2Cor 10:5 (thought)
- **Observation 22596** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Death words and the supposition meet in seven verses. In Gen 26:9, Job 29:18 and Jon 1:6 the dying or perishing is 2 words after the member and is the content of the thought; in Joh 11:13 death is 3 words before and the disciples suppose something else; in Act 8:20 perishing is 4 before, in 1Cor 4:9 death is 7 after and in 1Sa 20:3 it is 15 after. What is feared or foreseen as death is the commonest matter supposed.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Gen 26:9 (thought), 1Sa 20:3 (thinks), Job 29:18 (thought), Jon 1:6 (thought), Joh 11:13 (thought), Act 8:20 (thought), 1Cor 4:9 (think)
- **Observation 22597** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Enduring and the supposition meet in four verses: 'withstand' is 1 word after the thought in 2Ch 13:8, 'remain' is 9 after in 1Cor 7:26 and 20 before in Jer 40:5, and 'last' is 6 after in 1Cor 4:9. In 2Ch 13:8 and 1Cor 7:26 the holding out or remaining is the content of the supposition or judgment.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Ch 13:8 (think), Jer 40:5 (think), 1Cor 4:9 (think), 1Cor 7:26 (think)
- **Observation 22598** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Escaping and the supposition meet in four verses: 9 words after in Gen 32:8, 4 after in Est 4:13, 2 after in Act 16:27 and 10 after in Rom 2:3. In every verse the escape is part of what is supposed: hoped for by Jacob, denied to Esther, assumed by the jailer and put to the judge as a question.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 32:8 (thinking), Est 4:13 (think), Act 16:27 (supposing), Rom 2:3 (suppose)
- **Observation 22599** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Evil and the wicked stand with the supposition in six verses. In Mat 9:4 'evil' is 1 word after the member and is what is thought; in 1Cor 14:20, Psa 64:5 and 1Ti 6:5 it or 'depraved' is 2 to 5 words before; in Pro 21:29 the wicked is 5 words before and is set against the upright who gives thought; in 2Sa 14:17 it is 14 after. The supposition is held by, or about, those described as evil.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): 2Sa 14:17 (thought), Psa 64:5 (thinking), Pro 21:29 (thought), Mat 9:4 (think), 1Cor 14:20 (thinking), 1Ti 6:5 (imagining)
- **Observation 22600** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Fear and the supposition meet in six verses. In Gen 31:31, Exo 2:14, 2Sa 14:15 and Neh 6:9 the fear stands 1 to 2 words before the thought and the thought arises from it; in Luk 24:37 the startled and frightened are 3 and 1 words before; in Gen 20:11 the fear of God is 3 words after, as what the thought says is absent. Fear precedes the supposition in five of the six.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Gen 20:11 (thought), Gen 31:31 (thought), Exo 2:14 (thought), 2Sa 14:15 (thought), Neh 6:9 (thinking), Luk 24:37 (thought)
- **Observation 22601** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Folly and the supposition meet in five verses: 'simple' 4 words before in Pro 14:15, 'foolish' 2 after in Rom 1:21, 'fool' 7 after in 1Cor 3:18, 'foolish' 2 and 'fool' 9 after in 2Cor 11:16 and 'fool' 8 before in 2Cor 12:6. Folly is what the thinking is judged to be, becomes, or is asked not to be taken for.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Pro 14:15 (thought), Rom 1:21 (thinking), 1Cor 3:18 (thinks), 2Cor 11:16 (think), 2Cor 12:6 (think)
- **Observation 22602** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Security and the supposition meet in four verses: 'security' is 4 and 3 words after the thought of Hezekiah in 2Ki 20:19 and Isa 39:8, 'fortified' is 2 words before the thought of Sennacherib in 2Ch 32:1, and 'guard' is 4 after in Jer 40:5. In the first two security is the content of the thought; in the third the fortified cities are what he plans against.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Ki 20:19 (thought), 2Ch 32:1 (thinking), Isa 39:8 (thought), Jer 40:5 (think)
- **Observation 22603** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Two kinds of partner meet the supposition. The word 'surely' (flagged in #2536 as not a word of hearing) stands 1 to 5 words after the member in five Hebrew verses, inside the quoted thought; hearing proper stands 2 words after in Mat 6:7 (they think they will be heard), 12 before in Luk 8:18 and 7 after in 2Cor 12:6. Only Mat 6:7 ties hearing to the supposition.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Exo 2:14 (thought), Judg 3:24 (thought), 1Sa 16:6 (thought), 1Sa 20:26 (thought), 2Ki 5:11 (thought), Mat 6:7 (think), Luk 8:18 (thinks), 2Cor 12:6 (think)
- **Observation 22604** (layer-3, tag statement, marker inner_being, status draft): The heart or mind stands with the supposition in ten verses, within three words in Deu 15:9 and 2Sa 13:33 (1), Isa 10:7 (2), Eze 20:32 and Rom 1:21 (3), and at 4 to 10 in 1Ch 28:9, Mat 9:4, 1Ti 6:5, Eze 11:5 and Jam 1:26. The verses place the supposition in the heart or mind of the one who holds it, and in Isa 10:7 set what the heart plans against what it thinks.
  - applies to 10 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought), 2Sa 13:33 (suppose), 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Isa 10:7 (think), Eze 11:5 (think), Eze 20:32 (thought), Mat 9:4 (think), Rom 1:21 (thinking), 1Ti 6:5 (imagining), Jam 1:26 (thinks)
- **Observation 22605** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Honor and its opposite stand with the supposition in six verses: 'honoring' 4 words after in 2Sa 10:3 and 1Ch 19:3, 'worthy' 16 after in Act 13:25, 'honor' 9 before in Rom 1:21, 'less honorable' 1 and 'honor' 3 after in 1Cor 12:23, and disgrace 6 after in 1Ti 3:7. The supposition concerns what is honorable or reputed in the verses nearest the member.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): 2Sa 10:3 (think), 1Ch 19:3 (think), Act 13:25 (suppose), Rom 1:21 (thinking), 1Cor 12:23 (think), 1Ti 3:7 (thought)
- **Observation 22606** (layer-3, tag statement, marker inner_being, status draft): Intending words and the supposition meet in six verses. In 1Ch 28:9 (plan, 1 before), Psa 64:5 (purpose, 4 before) and Isa 10:7 (intend, 4 before) the partner is of the same kind as the member; in Joh 11:13 'meant' is 2 words after and is what Jesus meant, which they supposed otherwise; in Act 27:13 and Jam 4:5 'purpose' is 2 and 1 after. The supposition and the purpose are set together.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Psa 64:5 (thinking), Isa 10:7 (think), Joh 11:13 (thought), Act 27:13 (supposing), Jam 4:5 (suppose)
- **Observation 22607** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Killing and the supposition meet in six verses: 1 word after in 2Sa 13:32 and 2Sa 21:16, 2 words before in Luk 13:4, Joh 16:2 and Act 16:27, and 4 to 5 before in Exo 2:14. In Joh 16:2 the killer supposes he serves God and in Act 16:27 the jailer supposes the prisoners have escaped; the killing is what is supposed or intended.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Exo 2:14 (thought), 2Sa 13:32 (suppose), 2Sa 21:16 (thought), Luk 13:4 (think), Joh 16:2 (think), Act 16:27 (supposing)
- **Observation 22608** (layer-3, tag statement, marker evidence, status draft): Knowing and the supposition meet in thirteen verses with different ties. In 1Cor 8:2 (1 to 8 words after) and Ecc 9:10 (1 after) knowledge is what the person thinks he has or what is absent; in Mat 9:4 and Eze 11:5 the thinking is known by another; elsewhere the knowing is 3 to 15 words away in separate clauses (Exo 2:14, Rut 4:4, 2Ki 7:12, 1Ch 28:9, Act 12:9, Rom 1:21, Rom 14:14, 2Cor 10:5).
  - applies to 13 occurrence(s): Exo 2:14 (thought), Rut 4:4 (thought), 1Sa 20:3 (thinks), 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Ecc 9:10 (thought), Eze 11:5 (think), Mat 9:4 (think), Act 12:9 (thought), Rom 1:21 (thinking), Rom 14:14 (thinks), 1Cor 8:2 (imagines); and 1 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 22609** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Work stands with the supposition in four verses: 4 words after in Neh 6:9 (the work that will not be done), 1 before in Ecc 9:10 (no work or thought in Sheol), 11 after in Mat 21:28 and 3 after in Eph 3:20 (the power at work beyond what we think). Work is the thing the thought is about or set beside, not a tie of grammar.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Neh 6:9 (thinking), Ecc 9:10 (thought), Mat 21:28 (think), Eph 3:20 (think)
- **Observation 22610** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Crying and weeping stand with the supposition in four verses: 'cry' 14 words after in Deu 15:9, 'cried out' 4 after in Mar 6:49, weeping 6 after in Joh 11:31 and weeping before in Joh 20:15. In the three Gospel verses the crying or weeping follows or accompanies a supposition about a ghost, the tomb or the gardener.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought), Mar 6:49 (thought), Joh 11:31 (supposing), Joh 20:15 (supposing)
- **Observation 22611** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Life and death words stand with the supposition in eight verses. 'Dead' is 9, 4, 2 and 7 words after in 2Sa 13:32, 2Sa 13:33, Act 14:19 and Act 26:8, and in the first three the supposition is that someone is dead; 'alive' or 'lives' is 5 to 11 words after in 2Ki 7:12, 1Sa 20:3, 1Sa 27:11 and Luk 13:4. The state of being alive or dead is the commonest object of the supposition in this pairing.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): 1Sa 20:3 (thinks), 1Sa 27:11 (thinking), 2Sa 13:32 (suppose), 2Sa 13:33 (suppose), 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), Luk 13:4 (think), Act 14:19 (supposing), Act 26:8 (thought)
- **Observation 22612** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Loyalty and steadfast love stand with the supposition in three verses: steadfast love is 1 word after the thought in Psa 48:9 and 3 after in Psa 94:18, and faithfulness is 10 before in 2Ch 32:1. In Psa 48:9 the Lord's love is what is thought on, and in Psa 94:18 it answers the thought that the foot slips.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 2Ch 32:1 (thinking), Psa 48:9 (thought), Psa 94:18 (thought)
- **Observation 22613** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Silence and rest stand with the supposition in four verses: 'rest' 5 words after in 2Sa 14:17, 'silent' 1 before in Psa 50:21, 'remains' 3 before in 1Cor 7:40 and 'ceased' 5 after in 1Pe 4:1. Only in Psa 50:21 is the silence the ground of the supposition, that the one who is silent is like the one who accuses.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Sa 14:17 (thought), Psa 50:21 (thought), 1Cor 7:40 (think), 1Pe 4:1 (thinking)
- **Observation 22614** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): A gift or presence stands with the supposition in four verses with different senses. In Gen 32:20 the present (3 words after) is the means of appeasing, and in Jer 40:5 the present (7 after) is a gift; in Rut 4:4 ('presence') and 1Cor 7:26 ('present distress') the English word renders a place and a time, flagged in #2536. Only Gen 32:20 ties a gift to the supposition.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 32:20 (thought), Rut 4:4 (thought), Jer 40:5 (think), 1Cor 7:26 (think)
- **Observation 22615** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Pain and hunger stand with the supposition in three verses: 'hungry' 6 words before in 2Ki 7:12, 'suffered' 10 after in Luk 13:2, and 'suffered' 5 before and 2 after in 1Pe 4:1. In 1Pe 4:1 the suffering of Christ is within two words of the way of thinking the readers are to arm themselves with.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), Luk 13:2 (think), 1Pe 4:1 (thinking)
- **Observation 22616** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Answering and the supposition meet in nine verses: the four of Gen 18, where 'answered' is 4 words after each supposed number, Luk 7:43 (1 before), Luk 13:2 (2 before), Est 4:13 (3 before), Gen 31:31 (6 before) and Exo 2:14 (13 before). In every verse the supposition is said inside a spoken exchange of question and reply.
  - applies to 9 occurrence(s): Gen 18:29 (suppose), Gen 18:30 (suppose), Gen 18:31 (suppose), Gen 18:32 (suppose), Gen 31:31 (thought), Exo 2:14 (thought), Est 4:13 (think), Luk 7:43 (suppose), Luk 13:2 (think)
- **Observation 22617** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Peace stands with the supposition in four verses: 2 to 4 words after in 2Ki 20:19, Isa 39:8, Mat 10:34 and Luk 12:51. The same word has two stances in the verses: Hezekiah supposes there will be peace in his days, and in Mat 10:34 and Luk 12:51 the supposition that Jesus came to bring peace is the one corrected.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 2Ki 20:19 (thought), Isa 39:8 (thought), Mat 10:34 (think), Luk 12:51 (think)
- **Observation 22618** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Returning stands with the supposition in three verses: 5 words after in Jer 3:7 (expected and not done), 8 after in Heb 11:15 (what thinking of the land would have allowed) and 19 before in Jer 40:5. In Jer 3:7 and Heb 11:15 the return is what the thought leads toward and in Jer 3:7 it does not happen.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Jer 3:7 (thought), Jer 40:5 (think), Heb 11:15 (thinking)
- **Observation 22619** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Reward and its opposite stand with the supposition in three verses: 'unworthy' 1 word before in Deu 15:9 (flagged in #2536 as stored under reward), 'pay' 2 after in Mat 22:17 and 'punishment' 1 before in Heb 10:29. In the two Gospel and Hebrew verses the thought asked of the hearer is about what is owed or what is deserved.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought), Mat 22:17 (think), Heb 10:29 (think)
- **Observation 22620** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The poor and the rich stand with the supposition in five verses: 'poor' 10 words after in Deu 15:9, 'poor' 3 and 'needy' 2 before in Psa 40:17, 'rich' 4 before in Mic 2:8, 'poor' 17 after in Joh 13:29 and 'abundantly' 6 before in Eph 3:20. In Psa 40:17 and Deu 15:9 the poor are the ones the thought is directed to; in Joh 13:29 the disciples suppose a gift to the poor.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought), Psa 40:17 (thought), Mic 2:8 (thought), Joh 13:29 (thought), Eph 3:20 (think)
- **Observation 22621** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Righteousness, truth and the lawful stand with the supposition in six verses: 'just' 2 words after in Job 35:2, 'lawful' 1 after in Mat 22:17, 'truth' 5 before in 2Cor 12:6 and 1 before in 1Ti 6:5, 'honorable' and 'just' 15 and 13 before in Phili 4:8, and 'righteous' 3 after in Gen 18:28. In Job 35:2 and Mat 22:17 the supposition is the judgment whether something is just or lawful.

- **Observation 22622** (layer-3, tag statement, marker evidence, status draft): Searching and finding stand with the supposition in ten verses. In Gen 18:28 and 18:30 'find' is the condition of each supposed number; in Joh 20:15 the seeking is 1 word before the supposition; in Joh 5:39 the search of the Scriptures is 4 words before; in Mat 18:12 (17 after), Luk 2:44 (9 after), 2Sa 10:3 and 1Ch 19:3 the search is in a later clause; in 1Ch 28:9 the Lord searches hearts 6 words before.
  - applies to 10 occurrence(s): Gen 18:28 (suppose), Gen 18:30 (suppose), 2Sa 10:3 (think), 1Ch 19:3 (think), 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Ecc 9:10 (thought), Mat 18:12 (think), Luk 2:44 (supposing), Joh 5:39 (think), Joh 20:15 (supposing)
- **Observation 22623** (layer-3, tag statement, marker evidence, status draft): Doing and serving stand with the supposition in twelve verses. 'Do' is the partner in nine verses (ten words, two of them in Num 33:56): Num 33:56 (1 after, 3 before), Luk 12:17 (4 after), Gen 18:29 and 18:30 (6 and 9 after), Rom 2:3, 1Cor 7:36, Eze 18:14, Ecc 9:10, Eph 3:20; 'serve' is 12 words before in 1Ch 28:9, 'service' is 2 words after in Joh 16:2 and 'minister' 16 after in Phili 2:25. - **Observation 22624** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Giving stands with the supposition in twelve verses. - **Observation 22625** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Sin stands with the supposition in five verses: 'sins' 5 words after in Eze 18:14, 'sinners' 5 after in Luk 13:2, 'sin' 6 after in 1Pe 4:1, 15 after in 1Cor 7:36 and 19 after in Deu 15:9. In Luk 13:2 and Eze 18:14 the sin is within the clause of what is supposed; in the other three it is in a later clause.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought), Eze 18:14 (suppose), Luk 13:2 (think), 1Cor 7:36 (thinks), 1Pe 4:1 (thinking)
- **Observation 22626** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Taking stands with the supposition in seven verses. In Luk 8:18 (taken, 2 after) and Act 8:20 (obtain, 1 after) the taking is what the supposed possession or what Simon supposed he could do; in 2Cor 10:5 'take' is 2 words before and acts on thought; in 2Sa 13:33, 2Ki 7:12, Mat 17:25 and Joh 20:15 it is a different act 4 to 17 words from the member.

- **Observation 22627** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Asking stands with the supposition in three verses: 'pray' 6 words before in Mat 6:7, 'appeal' 3 after in Mat 26:53 and 'request' 7 after in 2Sa 14:15. In Mat 6:7 those who pray suppose they will be heard for many words; in Mat 26:53 the supposition is whether Jesus cannot appeal to the Father.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 2Sa 14:15 (thought), Mat 6:7 (think), Mat 26:53 (think)
- **Observation 22628** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Affliction stands with the supposition in three verses: 'afflict' 1 word after in Phili 1:17, 'distress' 3 after in 1Cor 7:26 and 'affliction' 8 after in Lam 1:9. In Phili 1:17 the thought is of afflicting Paul and the affliction is 1 word after it; in 1Cor 7:26 the distress is the ground of the judgment.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Lam 1:9 (thought), 1Cor 7:26 (think), Phili 1:17 (thinking)
- **Observation 22629** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Trust stands with the supposition in four verses: 'trusted' 2 words before in 1Sa 27:12, 'trustingly' 1 before in Mic 2:8, and 'trust' 8 after in 2Ki 18:20 and Isa 36:5. In 1Sa 27:12 Achish's trust is the ground of his thought, and in the two parallel verses the question whom they trust follows the supposed claim about words.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 1Sa 27:12 (thinking), 2Ki 18:20 (think), Isa 36:5 (think), Mic 2:8 (thought)
- **Observation 22630** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Cleanness and uncleanness stand with the supposition in three verses: 'unclean' 2 words before and after and 7 before in Rom 14:14, 'clean' 4 and 7 after in 1Sa 20:26 and 'uncleanness' 5 before in Lam 1:9. In Rom 14:14 the uncleanness is the object of the thinking and depends on it; in 1Sa 20:26 Saul's thought is that David is not clean.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 1Sa 20:26 (thought), Lam 1:9 (thought), Rom 14:14 (thinks)
- **Observation 22631** (layer-3, tag statement, marker evidence, status draft): Wisdom and understanding stand with the supposition in eight verses. The partner is within two words in Pro 14:15 (prudent, 1 before), Psa 73:16 (understand, 1 after), Ecc 9:10 (wisdom, 2 after) and 1Cor 3:18 (wise, 2 after), where the person thinks that he is wise; in 1Ch 28:9 (3 before), 2Sa 14:17 (12 after), Rom 2:3 (11 after) and 1Cor 7:40 (7 before) the partner is farther off.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): 2Sa 14:17 (thought), 1Ch 28:9 (thought), Psa 73:16 (thought), Pro 14:15 (thought), Ecc 9:10 (thought), Rom 2:3 (suppose), 1Cor 3:18 (thinks), 1Cor 7:40 (think)
- **Observation 22632** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Destruction words and the attending meet in three verses. In Psa 38:12 the ruin is 1 word before the meditating and the meditating is on treachery; in Psa 119:78 the speaker meditates on the precepts after being wronged (3 words before); in Job 2:3 the destroying is 18 words after the Lord's considering of Job, in a later clause. Harm stands beside the attending in the Psalm verses, as its object or its occasion.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 2:3 (considered), Psa 38:12 (meditate), Psa 119:78 (meditate)
- **Observation 22633** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Evil and the wicked meet the attending in three verses. In Job 1:8 and Job 2:3 the evil that Job turns from stands 12 and 13 words after the Lord's considering, as part of the description of the one considered; in Pro 15:28 the wicked is 3 words after the heart of the righteous that ponders, and is set against it. The evil belongs to the one described or contrasted, not to the act of attending.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 1:8 (considered), Job 2:3 (considered), Pro 15:28 (ponders)
- **Observation 22634** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Fear meets the attending in four verses: the fear of God is 9 and 10 words after the Lord's considering of Job in Job 1:8 and Job 2:3 and describes him, in Job 15:4 fear and meditation are both hindered (2 words), and in Mat 1:20 fear is 14 words after Joseph's considering, in the angel's words. The fear of God is the standing of the one considered or what meditation is set beside.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Job 1:8 (considered), Job 2:3 (considered), Job 15:4 (meditation), Mat 1:20 (considered)
- **Observation 22635** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Remembering and the attending are paired in four verses, always 3 words apart with the remembering first: Psa 63:6, Psa 77:3, Psa 77:6 and Psa 143:5. The remembering of God, a song or the days of old comes first and the meditating follows in the same sentence, so the two are one movement: a recalling and then a going over.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 63:6 (meditate), Psa 77:3 (meditate), Psa 77:6 (meditate), Psa 143:5 (meditate)
- **Observation 22636** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Precepts and authority meet the attending in four verses. In Psa 119:15, 119:27 and 119:78 the precepts are within two words of the meditating and are its object; in Mar 10:42 those who exercise authority stand 8 words after the members reputed to rule, so the reputed ruling and the exercise of authority are set together. The precepts are the thing meditated on; authority is the standing counted.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 119:15 (meditate), Psa 119:27 (meditate), Psa 119:78 (meditate), Mar 10:42 (considered)
- **Observation 22637** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The heart meets the attending in five verses within three words: 1 word after the meditation in Psa 19:14 and Psa 49:3, 2 after in Psa 77:6, 2 before in Pro 15:28 and 3 after in Luk 2:19. In every verse the heart is the place of the meditating or pondering, which is one of the relations found across the subgroups (the heart as place).
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Psa 19:14 (meditation), Psa 49:3 (meditation), Psa 77:6 (meditate), Pro 15:28 (ponders), Luk 2:19 (pondering)
- **Observation 22638** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Works meet the attending in four verses. In Psa 77:12 (1 word before), Psa 143:5 (4 after) and Psa 145:5 (1 before) the works are God's and are the object of the meditating; in Ecc 2:11 the toil (4 after) is the speaker's own, considered. The attending is directed to God's works in three and to one's own work in one.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Psa 77:12 (meditate), Psa 143:5 (meditate), Psa 145:5 (meditate), Ecc 2:11 (considered)
- **Observation 22639** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Life and death meet the attending in four verses: 'living' 5 words after in Isa 53:8, 'live' 8 after in Eze 18:28 (life as the outcome of considering and turning away), and 'dead' 4 and 7 after in Rom 4:19 and Heb 11:19 (a body as good as dead and the raising of the dead as what is considered). The considered matter is death in two verses and the result is life in one.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Isa 53:8 (considered), Eze 18:28 (considered), Rom 4:19 (considered), Heb 11:19 (considered)
- **Observation 22640** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Love meets the attending in two verses: 'love' is 1 word before the meditating in Psa 119:48 and 3 before in Psa 119:97, where the love of the commandments or the law comes first and the meditation follows. In Jos 7:21 the partner is covetousness 3 words after the surface 'weighing', which is a weight of gold there and not an attending.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Jos 7:21 (weighing), Psa 119:48 (meditate), Psa 119:97 (meditation)
- **Observation 22641** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Searching meets the attending in three verses: 'search' is 4 words after the meditating in Psa 77:6, 'seek' 7 and 4 before in Psa 38:12, and 'looking for' 8 after the pondering in Act 10:19. In Psa 77:6 the spirit's search follows the meditating; in Act 10:19 the men looking for Peter are named while he ponders the vision.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Psa 38:12 (meditate), Psa 77:6 (meditate), Act 10:19 (pondering)
- **Observation 22642** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Taking meets the surface 'weighing' in three verses where it is a weight of gold or a balance: 'took' 3 words before in Gen 24:22, 4 after in Jos 7:21 and 'take' 2 and 13 before in Eze 5:1; only in Mat 1:20 does 'take' (15 words after) belong to Joseph's considering, in the words 'do not fear to take Mary'. The weights are not acts of the mind and are read as standalone.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 24:22 (weighing), Jos 7:21 (weighing), Eze 5:1 (weighing), Mat 1:20 (considered)
- **Observation 22643** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Wisdom and understanding meet the attending in seven verses. A second verb of musing stands in the same clause in Psa 77:12 and Psa 143:5 (3 words); in Psa 49:3 the meditation lies between wisdom (1 before) and understanding (2 after); in Pro 17:28 the silent fool is considered wise (1 after) and intelligent (4); in Psa 119:27 understanding is 3 before, in Ecc 12:9 'wise' is 6 before the weighing and in Isa 53:8 'judgment' is 4 before.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Psa 49:3 (meditation), Psa 77:12 (meditate), Psa 119:27 (meditate), Psa 143:5 (meditate), Pro 17:28 (considered), Ecc 12:9 (weighing), Isa 53:8 (considered)
- **Observation 22644** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Believing meets the reasoning in four verses. In Act 19:9 the unbelief is 10 words before Paul's reasoning, as what the reasoning meets; in 1Th 3:5, 1Th 3:7 and 1Ti 1:16 faith or believing is 8 to 13 words after the phrase 'for this reason', as what the ground leads to. The reasoning answers unbelief, and the stated ground is followed by faith.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Act 19:9 (reasoning), 1Th 3:5 (reason), 1Th 3:7 (reason), 1Ti 1:16 (reason)
- **Observation 22645** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Words of duration stand 7 to 17 words from the reasoning or the ground in three verses (Dan 4:34, 1Ti 1:16, 1Pe 3:15), in other clauses and phrases (the everlasting kingdom, eternal life, always being prepared). The two meet only in that the verse says something is lasting beside the ground; no tie of grammar joins them.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Dan 4:34 (reason), 1Ti 1:16 (reason), 1Pe 3:15 (reason)
- **Observation 22646** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Bearing meets the ground in three verses: 'bear' is 2 words before the ground in Num 18:32 (bear no sin by reason of it) and 2 after in 1Th 3:5 (could bear it no longer), and 'patience' is 9 after in 1Ti 1:16. The ground is stated in the same clause as the bearing in the first two.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Num 18:32 (reason), 1Th 3:5 (reason), 1Ti 1:16 (reason)
- **Observation 22647** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Knowing meets the reasoning and the ground in three verses. In Mar 12:24 the ground of the error is the lack of knowing (2 words after the phrase of reason) and the negator reaches the knowing; in Luk 9:47 Jesus' knowing is 1 word before the reasoning in the disciples' hearts; in 1Th 3:5 learning is 6 after the ground. Knowing is the ground or the knower of the reasoning.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mar 12:24 (reason), Luk 9:47 (reasoning), 1Th 3:5 (reason)
- **Observation 22648** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Sin meets the ground and the reasoning in three verses: sin is 1 word before the reason in Num 18:32 (no sin borne by reason of the offering), 4 after the reasoning together in Isa 1:18, and 3 and 9 before the ground in 1Jo 3:8 (the devil sinning from the beginning). Sin is the matter the reasoning addresses or the ground that is stated.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Num 18:32 (reason), Isa 1:18 (reason), 1Jo 3:8 (reason)
- **Observation 22649** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Hearing meets the regard in three verses: listening is 8 words after in 2Ch 6:19 where the regard and the hearing are asked together, hearing is 4 before in Job 35:13 where an empty cry is neither heard nor regarded by God, and 6 before in Act 28:22. The word 'surely' in Job 35:13 is not a word of hearing (#2536). Hearing and regard are paired as what is given or withheld together.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 2Ch 6:19 (regard), Job 35:13 (regard), Act 28:22 (regard)
- **Observation 22650** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Works meet the regard in four verses: the Lord's works are 1 and 3 words after the regard in Psa 28:5 and 5 after in Isa 5:12, where they are what is not regarded; in Exo 5:9 the work (5 before) and labor (1 before) are the burden laid on the men, who are to pay no regard to lying words; in Lam 4:2 the work of a potter's hands (3 after) is what the sons of Zion are regarded as. The works are the object of the regard in the first two.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Exo 5:9 (regard), Psa 28:5 (regard), Isa 5:12 (regard), Lam 4:2 (regarded)
- **Observation 22651** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Worthlessness and conceit meet the regard in three verses: 'worthless' is 2 words after in 1Sa 1:16 (regard asked not to be given as to a worthless woman) and 1 after in 1Sa 25:25 (Nabal, the worthless fellow), and 'conceit' is 3 after in Job 37:24 (God does not regard the wise in their own conceit). The regard is refused or asked against a person described as worthless or conceited.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 1Sa 1:16 (regard), 1Sa 25:25 (regard), Job 37:24 (regard)
- **Observation 22652** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Contempt meets the regard in three verses: 'despised' 2 words before in Isa 33:8 (no regard for man), 'spoken against' 9 after in Act 28:22 and 'reviled' 11 after in 1Ti 6:1. In each verse a word of contempt is in the same sentence as the regard, either as what accompanies the lack of regard or as what the regard is asked to guard against.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Isa 33:8 (regard), Act 28:22 (regard), 1Ti 6:1 (regard)
- **Observation 22653** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): A change of color meets the thoughts in three Aramaic verses: 'changed' is 1 word before in Dan 5:6, 'change' 3 after in Dan 5:10 and 'changed' 5 after in Dan 7:28. In each verse the thoughts alarm the person and the bodily change is named within five words, so the thoughts and the visible effect are stated together.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Dan 5:6 (thoughts), Dan 5:10 (thoughts), Dan 7:28 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22654** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Evil and wickedness meet the thoughts in fourteen verses, within five words in each. Within one word: Pro 15:26, Mat 15:19, Mar 7:21, Jam 2:4; within three: Gen 6:5, Job 21:27, Psa 10:4, Psa 56:5; at four or five: Pro 12:5, Isa 55:7, Isa 59:7, Jer 4:14, Eze 38:10, Mat 9:4. In Pro 12:5 the thoughts of the righteous are set beside the counsels of the wicked.
  - applies to 14 occurrence(s): Gen 6:5 (thoughts), Job 21:27 (thoughts), Psa 10:4 (thoughts), Psa 56:5 (thoughts), Pro 12:5 (thoughts), Pro 15:26 (thoughts), Isa 55:7 (thoughts), Isa 59:7 (thoughts), Jer 4:14 (thoughts), Eze 38:10 (thoughts), Mat 9:4 (thoughts), Mat 15:19 (thoughts); and 2 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 22655** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Fear and alarm meet the thoughts in six verses. In Dan 4:19, 5:6, 5:10 and 7:28 the thoughts are the subject of alarming, the alarm 1 to 2 words after; in Job 32:6 the speaker is afraid 4 words before declaring his opinion; in 2Cor 11:3 the fear (10 before) is Paul's fear about the readers' thoughts. The thoughts cause alarm in four and stand beside fear in two.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Job 32:6 (opinion), Dan 4:19 (thoughts), Dan 5:6 (thoughts), Dan 5:10 (thoughts), Dan 7:28 (thoughts), 2Cor 11:3 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22656** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): A heart, mind or conscience is named with the thoughts in all fifteen verses. It is within three words in Gen 6:5, 1Ch 29:18, Dan 2:30, Luk 1:51, Eze 38:10, Mat 15:19 (3 before), Luk 2:35 and Heb 4:12, and 4 to 9 away in Psa 139:23, Mar 7:21, Jer 4:14, Mat 9:4, Luk 5:22 and Dan 7:28; in Rom 2:15 the conscience is 3 before. The thoughts are placed in the heart or mind, one of the relations found across the subgroups.
  - applies to 15 occurrence(s): Gen 6:5 (thoughts), 1Ch 29:18 (thoughts), Psa 139:23 (thoughts), Jer 4:14 (thoughts), Eze 38:10 (thoughts), Dan 2:30 (thoughts), Dan 7:28 (thoughts), Mat 9:4 (thoughts), Mat 15:19 (thoughts), Mar 7:21 (thoughts), Luk 1:51 (thoughts), Luk 2:35 (thoughts); and 3 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 22657** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Intention meets the thoughts in four verses, of the same kind as the member: 'intention' 1 word before in Gen 6:5, 'purposes' 1 before in 1Ch 29:18, 'plan' 4 after in Mic 4:12 and 'intentions' 2 after in Heb 4:12. Thoughts and intentions are paired, as what a person holds or what the Lord holds and discerns.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 6:5 (thoughts), 1Ch 29:18 (thoughts), Mic 4:12 (thoughts), Heb 4:12 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22658** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Knowing meets the thoughts in sixteen verses. In thirteen the thoughts are the object of a verb of knowing or perceiving, within four words before the member where the position is stored (Job 21:27, Psa 94:11, Psa 139:2, Psa 139:23, Dan 2:30, Mic 4:12, Mat 9:4, Mat 12:25, Luk 5:22, Luk 6:8, Luk 11:17, 1Cor 2:11, 1Cor 3:20); in Dan 2:29 it is 7 after, and in Mat 22:16 and Mar 12:14 'opinion' stands 14 and 9 words after 'know'. The knower is the Lord, Jesus or the Spirit in most.
  - applies to 16 occurrence(s): Job 21:27 (thoughts), Psa 94:11 (thoughts), Psa 139:2 (thoughts), Psa 139:23 (thoughts), Dan 2:29 (thoughts), Dan 2:30 (thoughts), Mic 4:12 (thoughts), Mat 9:4 (thoughts), Mat 12:25 (thoughts), Mat 22:16 (opinion), Mar 12:14 (opinion), Luk 5:22 (thoughts); and 4 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 22659** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Works meet the thoughts in three verses: the works are 3 words before in Psa 92:5 and 1 before in Isa 66:18, where God's works and God's thoughts, or the works and thoughts of the nations, are paired, and 10 before in Rom 2:15 where the law's work is set in the hearts. In the first two the pairing is of the Lord's works with his thoughts.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Psa 92:5 (thoughts), Isa 66:18 (thoughts), Rom 2:15 (conflicting thoughts)
- **Observation 22660** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Words of life meet the thoughts in three verses: 'living' 9 words before in Dan 2:30, 'live' 3 before in Dan 5:10 (live forever) and 'living' 19 before in Heb 4:12 (the word is living). The distances and the phrases show no tie of grammar between the living and the thoughts.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Dan 2:30 (thoughts), Dan 5:10 (thoughts), Heb 4:12 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22661** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Declaring meets the opinion and the thoughts in six verses. In Job 32:6, 32:10 and 32:17 'declare' is 2 words before the opinion and the opinion is what Elihu declares; in Psa 40:5 (proclaim, 4 after) and Isa 55:8 (declares, 4 after) the declaring follows the Lord's thoughts; in Dan 5:10 the queen declared 5 words before. The opinion is declared by the one who holds it and the thoughts are declared by the Lord.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Job 32:6 (opinion), Job 32:10 (opinion), Job 32:17 (opinion), Psa 40:5 (thoughts), Isa 55:8 (thoughts), Dan 5:10 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22662** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Answering meets the thoughts and the opinion in five verses. In Job 20:2 and Luk 5:22 the answer is 1 word after the thoughts, as their occasion; in Dan 4:19 the king answers 3 words after; in Job 32:6 and Job 32:17 the answer stands 12 and 6 words before the opinion is declared. The thoughts are the occasion of an answer or are given in one.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Job 20:2 (thoughts), Job 32:6 (opinion), Job 32:17 (opinion), Dan 4:19 (thoughts), Luk 5:22 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22663** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Wealth meets the thoughts in three verses: 'wealth' 6 words before the imagination in Pro 18:11 (the wall of the rich man's wealth), 'rich' 7 after in Ecc 10:20 (not to curse the rich even in your thoughts) and 'abundantly' 8 after in Isa 55:7. Only in Pro 18:11 is wealth the thing the imagination pictures.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Pro 18:11 (imagination), Ecc 10:20 (thoughts), Isa 55:7 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22664** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Righteousness meets the thoughts in four verses: 'righteous' 1 and 'just' 2 words after in Pro 12:5, 'lawful' 8 after in Mar 12:14, and sexual immorality 3 and 1 words after in Mat 15:19 and Mar 7:21, the next item in the list of what comes out of the heart. The thoughts of the righteous are set against the wicked's in Pro 12:5, and in the lists the evil thoughts head the sins.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Pro 12:5 (thoughts), Mat 15:19 (thoughts), Mar 7:21 (thoughts), Mar 12:14 (opinion)
- **Observation 22665** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Teaching meets the thoughts and the opinion in three verses: 'counsels' 3 words after in Pro 12:5, and 'teach' 9 before in Mat 22:16 and 5 after in Mar 12:14, where the opinion of persons is set against teaching the way of God. The counsels of the wicked are paired with the thoughts of the righteous, and the Teacher is said not to regard opinion.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Pro 12:5 (thoughts), Mat 22:16 (opinion), Mar 12:14 (opinion)
- **Observation 22666** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Wisdom and discernment meet the thoughts in six verses. In Psa 139:2 (discern, 1 before) and Heb 4:12 (discerning, 1 before) the thoughts are the object of discerning; in 1Cor 3:20 (wise, 1 after) the thoughts are of the wise and are futile; in Mic 4:12 (understand, 3 after), Pro 18:2 (understanding, 6 before) and Dan 2:30 (wisdom, 13 before) the partner is farther off.
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Psa 139:2 (thoughts), Pro 18:2 (opinion), Dan 2:30 (thoughts), Mic 4:12 (thoughts), 1Cor 3:20 (thoughts), Heb 4:12 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22667** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Calling meets the conceiving in three verses, after the conceiving and the bearing: 'call' is 3 words after in Isa 7:14, 5 after in Mat 1:23 and 7 after in Luk 1:31, where the child's name is called. The calling of the name follows the conceiving of a son; the verses are the bodily conceiving of a child and bear no inner-being meaning (researcher's ruling, #2527 v13).
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Isa 7:14 (conceive), Mat 1:23 (conceive), Luk 1:31 (conceive)
- **Observation 22668** (layer-3, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Bearing meets the conceiving in six verses: 1 word after in Jdg 13:3, 13:5, 13:7 and Isa 7:14, 2 after in Mat 1:23 and 4 after in Luk 1:31. In every verse the bearing is the second act of the pair, conceive and bear; the verses are the bodily conceiving of a child and bear no inner-being meaning (researcher's ruling, #2527 v13).
  - applies to 6 occurrence(s): Judg 13:3 (conceive), Judg 13:5 (conceive), Judg 13:7 (conceive), Isa 7:14 (conceive), Mat 1:23 (conceive), Luk 1:31 (conceive)
- **Observation 22104** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Num 11:12 and Judg 13:3 harah ('to conceive') is said of a woman's bearing of a child: Moses asks whether he conceived the people and gave them birth, a mother's acts set beside nursing, and the angel tells the barren woman, who has not borne children, that she will conceive and bear a son; conceiving is the beginning of the child.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Num 11:12 (conceive), Judg 13:3 (conceive)
- **Observation 22105** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 15:35, Isa 33:11 and Isa 59:4 harah ('to conceive') is not said of a child: what is conceived is trouble, chaff and mischief, and what is born of it is evil, stubble and iniquity; the verbs of pregnancy and birth are carried over to the devising of harm, the birth being the deed that follows the plan.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 15:35 (conceive), Isa 33:11 (conceive), Isa 59:4 (conceive)
- **Observation 22106** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 18:11 the rich man's wealth is called his strong city, a high wall in his imagination: the wall is in what he pictures to himself, not in fact, so maskit ('imagination, figure') names what a man holds in his own mind about his security.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 18:11 (imagination)
- **Observation 22107** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Ti 6:5 nomizo ('to suppose, hold as settled') is the opinion of people whose minds are depraved and who lack the truth, that godliness brings profit; the verse sets it beside the constant friction they cause and traces it to their depraved minds.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ti 6:5 (imagining)
- **Observation 22108** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 1:2 hagah ('to meditate', literally to mutter) takes the law of the Lord as its object and day and night as its time: the one whose delight is in that law keeps occupied with it without ceasing, so the word names a continual dwelling on the written law.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 1:2 (meditates)
- **Observation 22109** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 32:6, 32:10 and 32:17 Elihu speaks of declaring his deah ('knowledge, opinion'): in 32:6 he was timid to declare it to the aged, in 32:10 and 32:17 he asks to be heard and will answer with his share; the word is a view he holds and means to set out in speech, not a fact he reports.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 32:6 (opinion), Job 32:10 (opinion), Job 32:17 (opinion)
- **Observation 22110** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 15:28 the righteous man's heart ponders how to answer him who asks, set against the wicked man's mouth that pours out evil: hagah ('to meditate, mutter') is the thinking that comes before speaking, done in the heart, so the answer is weighed before it is given.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 15:28 (ponders)
- **Observation 22111** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 4:34 and Dan 4:36 manda ('knowledge, understanding') is what Nebuchadnezzar says returned to him: in 4:34 when he lifted his eyes to heaven, after which he blessed the Most High, and in 4:36 together with the glory of his kingdom, his majesty and splendor; it names the understanding he had lost and now has again.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Dan 4:34 (reason), Dan 4:36 (reason)
- **Observation 22112** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 119:52 the psalmist calls to mind the Lord's rules from of old and takes comfort: zakar ('to remember') is the recalling of what the Lord has ordained, and the verse gives its effect, comfort.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 119:52 (think)
- **Observation 22113** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 139:23 the psalmist asks God to search him, to know his heart and to try him and know his thoughts: sarappim ('disquieting thoughts') are what stirs within him, set parallel to the heart, and he asks that God himself test and know it.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 139:23 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22114** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 24:22, Jos 7:21 and Eze 5:1 mishqal ('weight') is a weight: a gold ring of half a shekel and two bracelets of ten shekels (Gen 24:22), a bar of gold of fifty shekels (Jos 7:21) and the balances for weighing the hair in the sign-act of Eze 5:1; in all four it is the weight of a thing, not an act of the mind.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 24:22 (weighing), Jos 7:21 (weighing), Eze 5:1 (weighing)
- **Observation 22115** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 1:23 the verb whose usual sense is 'to have, hold' stands in the future active, third singular, and carries 'become pregnant' only through the clause it stands in (the virgin, the bearing of a son that follows); the dictionary sense of the code is possession, not conception.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mat 1:23 (conceive)
- **Observation 22116** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Heb 11:11 the noun whose dictionary senses are 'foundation, a casting down' is the first member of a two-noun phrase that the verse gives as conceiving; it names the laying down of seed, and the lexicon itself lists 'conception in the womb' for this verse.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Heb 11:11 (conceive)
- **Observation 22117** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Heb 11:11 the noun 'seed' stands in the genitive, the second member of the phrase that is rendered to conceive (with the casting down, G2602, in the same unit); here it is the male seed, which the lexicon lists for this verse as semen virile.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Heb 11:11 (conceive)
- **Observation 22118** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 1:31 the verb is in the future indicative, second person, with 'in your womb' and the bearing of a son after it; its sense here is 'become pregnant', one of the senses the lexicon lists for the code (Lk 1:24, 31, 36; 2:21).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 1:31 (conceive)
- **Observation 22119** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jdg 13:5, Jdg 13:7 and Isa 7:14 the feminine adjective 'pregnant' is predicated of a woman and followed by the bearing of a son; the word names the state of being with child, and the ESV renders the announcement with the verb conceive.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Judg 13:5 (conceive), Judg 13:7 (conceive), Isa 7:14 (conceive)
- **Observation 22120** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Num 5:28 the Niphal (the passive stem, as the morph HVNq3fs shows) of the verb 'to sow' means to be made pregnant, the sense the lexicon lists for the Niphal; the verse sets it as the result for a woman found clean.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Num 5:28 (conceive)
- **Observation 22121** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 51:5 the Piel perfect, third feminine singular, of the verb 'to be hot' means to conceive in the sexual sense (the Piel sense the lexicon lists); its subject is the speaker's mother and its object the speaker, so it names the origin of his life.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 51:5 (conceive)
- **Observation 22122** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 2:15 the plural noun 'reasonings, thoughts' is the subject of the accusing or excusing that stands beside the conscience; the lexicon lists 'a thought, cogitation' for this verse, and the verse makes the thoughts the voices that judge the person.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 2:15 (conflicting thoughts)
- **Observation 22123** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mar 10:42 the participle of the verb 'to think' is used of those reputed to rule the nations; the sense is 'to be reckoned or held to be', a reputation held by others, and not the thinking of the subject, which is where this occurrence departs from the usual sense.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mar 10:42 (considered)
- **Observation 22124** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 1:20 the aorist participle of the verb 'to reflect on' states Joseph's deliberation over the matter before the angel appears; the lexicon's sense 'to revolve thoroughly in the mind, consider carefully' fits, and the object is the circumstances of Mary's pregnancy.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mat 1:20 (considered)
- **Observation 22125** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Heb 11:11 and Heb 11:26 the verb whose first sense is 'to lead' is used of a judgment reached: Sarah judged the one who promised faithful, Moses counted the reproach of Christ a greater wealth; the sense in both is 'to esteem, count, hold to be', the lexicon's 'to think, consider, count'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Heb 11:11 (considered), Heb 11:26 (considered)
- **Observation 22126** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 4:19 the aorist verb of attentive observation has Abraham's own body as its object; the lexicon's sense 'to observe, consider thoroughly, with intense attention' fits, and the verse sets this looking at the facts beside his unweakened faith.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 4:19 (considered)
- **Observation 22127** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Heb 11:19 the verb 'to reckon' is in the aorist middle participle and its object is a clause (that God was able to raise); the sense is to conclude by reckoning, reasoning to a conviction on the ground of God's character.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Heb 11:19 (considered)
- **Observation 22128** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 4:19 the negator 'not' is stored in the unit of the surface 'considered' but the ESV verse states no negation of the considering: the 'not' in the verse bears on weakening in faith, and the negator sits beside the word, not on it; adjacency is not scope (LX-01). The occurrence is read as the negator it is, with the reach left to the verse.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 4:19 (considered)
- **Observation 22129** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 7:8 the Aramaic verb 'to be' (perfect, first person) is not a word of thinking: it stands with the participle of 'contemplate' (H7920) in the same verse and supplies the continued being-at-it of the looking, 'I was considering'; the dictionary sense 'to be, come to pass' is the helper here.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 7:8 (considered)
- **Observation 22130** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Ki 10:21, 2Ch 9:20 and Neh 13:13 the Niphal of the verb 'to think, reckon' is passive: silver was not reckoned as anything in Solomon's days, and the men were reckoned reliable; the sense is 'to be accounted', the value or standing others assign.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 1Ki 10:21 (considered), 2Ch 9:20 (considered), Neh 13:13 (considered)
- **Observation 22131** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 17:28 the Niphal imperfect, third masculine singular, means 'is reckoned' (a fool who is silent is reckoned wise); the standing is assigned from outside the person, by what others observe of his silence.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 17:28 (considered)
- **Observation 22132** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 1:8, Job 2:3 and Pro 24:32 the noun 'heart' is the object-word of an idiom of setting the heart on something (the verbs set or put in the same verses, H7760K and H7896K), and the ESV renders the idiom as considered; the heart is the seat of the attention directed at Job or at the field.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 1:8 (considered), Job 2:3 (considered), Pro 24:32 (considered)
- **Observation 22133** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ecc 2:11 the Qal of the verb 'to turn' is followed by the preposition and the objects of the speaker's work: the turning is a turning of attention toward them, and the ESV renders it considered; it is a physical verb used of directing the mind.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 2:11 (considered)
- **Observation 22134** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Eze 18:28 the Qal narrative form of the verb 'to see' is the first of two acts (he considered, he turned away from the transgressions); the sense is 'to observe, consider', seeing taken in as grounds for the change of direction.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Eze 18:28 (considered)
- **Observation 22135** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 1:8 and Job 2:3 the verb 'to set, put' (perfect, second masculine singular) is joined with the noun heart in the same units: the idiom is 'set your heart', and it is what the Lord asks of Satan about Job; the physical verb of placing here directs attention.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 1:8 (considered), Job 2:3 (considered)
- **Observation 22136** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Isa 53:8 the Polel imperfect of the verb 'to muse' is put as a question about the speaker's generation: who gave thought to what happened to him; the sense is 'to reflect upon, take to heart', a deliberate musing that the generation did not do.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 53:8 (considered)
- **Observation 22137** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 24:32 the verb 'to set, put' (imperfect, first person) is joined with heart (H3820A, id 1281985 in the same verse): the speaker set his heart on what he saw in the field; it is the placing verb of the attention idiom, not a dictionary sense of thought.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 24:32 (considered)
- **Observation 22138** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 7:8 the Aramaic Ithpael participle 'contemplate' is the reflecting act of the speaker at the horns, carried with the helper verb 'to be' (H1934) in the same verse; the sense is attentive consideration.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 7:8 (considered)
- **Observation 22139** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 16:20 the Hiphil participle of the verb 'to be prudent' has the word (the instruction) as its object: the one who gives attention with insight to the word finds good; the sense is 'to give heed to with understanding', the lexicon's Hiphil 'to give attention to, consider'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 16:20 (gives thought)
- **Observation 22140** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 17:29 the noun 'thought, design' in the genitive qualifies the image made by art: it is the thinking that devises it; the lexicon lists 'the result of thought, invention, device' for this verse, and the verse sets human devising against the divine being.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:29 (imagination)
- **Observation 22141** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 8:2 the connective 'but, now' stands at the head of the clause with the verb 'think' (G1380, id 1484787, same unit); it is the connective that turns from the thought of 8:1 to the person who thinks he knows, and is read for that work (RG-07), not for any thinking.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 8:2 (imagines)
- **Observation 22142** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 8:2 the present verb 'to think' has the infinitive 'to know something' as its content: the thought is a self-estimate of his own knowledge, which the verse then contradicts; the sense is 'to suppose, imagine oneself to', the lexicon's 'think, imagine, suppose'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 8:2 (imagines)
- **Observation 22143** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 41:7 the Qal imperfect, third masculine plural, of the verb 'to think, plan' has the worst for the speaker as its object: the sense is 'to devise', the planning of harm by those who whisper together against him, the lexicon's 'to plan, devise'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 41:7 (imagine)
- **Observation 22144** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jos 1:8, Psa 63:6 and Psa 143:5 the verb 'to mutter, meditate' is used of reflection on an object the verse names: the Book of the Law by day and night, God on the bed, God's works of old; the sense is a repeated, quiet musing on that object.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Jos 1:8 (meditate), Psa 63:6 (meditate), Psa 143:5 (meditate)
- **Observation 22145** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 38:12 the same verb stands in a clause with speaking of ruin: those who seek the speaker's hurt meditate treachery; the object is deceit and the sense is devising harm, which departs from the musing on the Lord seen in the other three verses.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 38:12 (meditate)
- **Observation 22146** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 24:63 the Qal infinitive 'to meditate' (the lexicon marks the meaning uncertain) states what Isaac went out to do in the field toward evening; the sense is quiet reflection while walking, set before the sight of the camels.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 24:63 (meditate)
- **Observation 22147** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 77:6, Psa 77:12, Psa 119:15, 119:23, 119:27, 119:48, 119:78, 119:148 and Psa 145:5 the verb 'to muse' is used of the speaker's reflection on an object the verse names: his song, God's work and deeds, the precepts, statutes, promise and wondrous works; the sense is a deliberate pondering of that object.
  - applies to 9 occurrence(s): Psa 77:6 (meditate), Psa 77:12 (meditate), Psa 119:15 (meditate), Psa 119:23 (meditate), Psa 119:27 (meditate), Psa 119:48 (meditate), Psa 119:78 (meditate), Psa 119:148 (meditate), Psa 145:5 (meditate)
- **Observation 22148** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 77:3 the same verb stands after remembering God and before the spirit faints; the lexicon lists 'to complain' for the Qal and the verse sets it with moaning, so it is musing that is a complaint, not a study of a named object.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 77:3 (meditate)
- **Observation 22149** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 49:3 the noun 'meditation' (construct, with the heart) is set in parallel with the mouth's speech of wisdom; it is the inner musing of the heart, and the verse says its content will be understanding.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 49:3 (meditation)
- **Observation 22150** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 19:14 the noun 'meditation' (construct, with the heart) is set in parallel with the words of the mouth and both are asked to be acceptable; it is the inner musing counterpart to speech, here chosen from the lexicon's 'meditation' over its 'resounding music'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 19:14 (meditation)
- **Observation 22151** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 104:34 the noun 'meditation' with the possessive stands as what the speaker offers and asks to be pleasing to the Lord; it is the musing as an offering, set before the speaker's rejoicing in the Lord.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 104:34 (meditation)
- **Observation 22152** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 15:4 the noun is the reflection before God that the speaker is accused of hindering; in Psa 119:97 and Psa 119:99 it is the law and the testimonies that are the speaker's meditation all day; the sense in all three is sustained reflection, whose object in the Psalm verses is the law.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 15:4 (meditation), Psa 119:97 (meditation), Psa 119:99 (meditation)
- **Observation 22153** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 8:6 the noun 'frame of thought, mind-set' appears twice in the same verse, once for the flesh and once for the Spirit, as what is death or life and peace; the lexicon lists 'frame of thought, aspirations' for this verse, and here it is the orientation itself.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 8:6 (mind on)
- **Observation 22154** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 8:5 the present verb 'to think, set one's mind on' is used of those who live by the flesh or by the Spirit, with the things of each as object; the sense is 'to be minded toward, set the mind on', the directing of the thought.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 8:5 (minds on)
- **Observation 22155** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Ki 18:27 the noun 'musing' is the first of Elijah's mocking alternatives about the god (musing, relieving himself, journey, sleep); it names preoccupation, being occupied in thought, and is a mocking use that departs from the devout musing of the Psalm verses.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ki 18:27 (musing)
- **Observation 22156** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 5:16 the perfect verb 'to know' has Christ as object and the phrase 'according to the flesh' as its standard: the sense is to have known and sized up in that way, a settled judgment of him; the ESV renders it regard. The pick-up note on this word is checked against the verse and stands.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 5:16 (once regarded)
- **Observation 22157** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 22:16 and Mar 12:14 the verb 'to look at' is negated and its object is the persons: the sense is 'to look to, have regard for' what a person appears to be, which the lexicon lists for Mt 22:16 and which the ESV renders as caring about opinion; the verse adds 'not swayed by appearances'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 22:16 (opinion), Mar 12:14 (opinion)
- **Observation 22158** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 18:2 the noun 'heart' is what the fool takes pleasure in laying bare: the ESV renders it his opinion, which is the inner view disclosed; the word is the heart as the holder of what he thinks.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 18:2 (opinion)
- **Observation 22159** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 10:19 the present participle of the verb 'to reflect on' states Peter's continuing reflection on the vision as the Spirit speaks; the sense is sustained pondering of a given object (the vision), the lexicon's 'to ponder, reflect'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 10:19 (pondering)
- **Observation 22160** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 2:19 the present participle of the verb 'to throw together' is joined with treasuring up and with the heart: Mary brings the events together in her mind, which is the lexicon's 'to ponder'; the place is stated, in her heart.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 2:19 (pondering)
- **Observation 22161** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 5:21 the Piel participle with the paths of a man as object means 'to weigh, make level', so the Lord weighs all his paths; the stored sense head 'to envy' for this code disagrees with the lexicon range (weigh, make level) and with the verse, and is flagged as a data error (RG-16), not corrected in the reading.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 5:21 (ponders)
- **Observation 22162** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 17:2, 17:17, 18:4, 18:19 and 24:25 the verb 'to discourse' is used of Paul reasoning with hearers: from the Scriptures in the synagogue (17:2), with Jews and devout persons (17:17), every Sabbath persuading (18:4), with the Jews (18:19), and before Felix on righteousness, self-control and judgment (24:25); the sense is spoken reasoning to persuade.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Act 17:2 (reasoned), Act 17:17 (reasoned), Act 18:4 (reasoned), Act 18:19 (reasoned), Act 24:25 (reasoned)
- **Observation 22163** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 13:11 the imperfect verb 'to reckon' is set with speaking and thinking as a childish way of working things out ('I reasoned like a child'); the sense is the process of reckoning something out, with the man's putting it away after.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 13:11 (reasoned)
- **Observation 22164** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 19:8 and Act 19:9 the present participle of the verb 'to discourse' states how Paul spoke in the synagogue for three months and then daily in the hall of Tyrannus, with persuading beside it and the kingdom of God as the subject; the sense is spoken reasoning with hearers.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Act 19:8 (reasoning), Act 19:9 (reasoning)
- **Observation 22165** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 9:47 the noun 'reasoning, thought' is what Jesus knows as being in their hearts after the dispute of 9:46; the lexicon lists thought and argument for this word, and here it is the inward line of thought, held in the heart and not spoken.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 9:47 (reasoning)
- **Observation 22166** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 10:21, Act 28:20 and 2Ti 1:6 the noun 'cause' is the ground on which something is done or asked: the cause of their coming, and 'for this reason' in Paul's request to see the Jews and in his reminder to Timothy; the sense is the ground or cause.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Act 10:21 (reason), Act 28:20 (reason), 2Ti 1:6 (reason)
- **Observation 22167** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 28:18 the noun stands in a legal setting, where the Romans found no ground for the death penalty after examining Paul; the lexicon lists the legal sense 'charge, case, ground for accusation'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 28:18 (reason)
- **Observation 22168** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 15:22 the inferential conjunction 'therefore, for this reason' ties Paul's having been hindered from coming to the work he has just described; it is a connective, read for the cause it states (RG-07), and carries no thinking of its own.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 15:22 (reason)
- **Observation 22169** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jam 3:17 the adjective 'compliant, easily persuaded' describes the wisdom from above in a list of its qualities; the lexicon lists this verse, and the sense is readiness to be persuaded, the ESV's 'open to reason'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 3:17 (reason)
- **Observation 22170** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Pe 3:15 the noun whose usual sense is 'word' is the thing asked for by the one who asks about the hope: an account, a reason given; the verse sets it with being prepared to make a defense, so here the word is an explanation and departs from the sense of a spoken word.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Pe 3:15 (reason)
