- **Observation 22171** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jos 5:4 and 1Ki 11:27 the noun 'word, matter' stands in 'this is the reason why': it is the matter that accounts for what follows, an explanation of a cause; the usual senses speech and thing are not the sense here.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Jos 5:4 (reason), 1Ki 11:27 (reason)
- **Observation 22172** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Eze 19:10 the stored word is the Qal perfect, third feminine singular, of the verb 'to be', the copula of 'your mother was like a vine'; it is not a word of reasoning and does not render 'by reason of' (which belongs to the preposition and the noun in the clause). The alignment into the unit 'reason' is flagged as a data error (RG-16), not corrected in the reading.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Eze 19:10 (reason)
- **Observation 22173** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 1:9 and Pro 3:30 the adverb 'for nothing, without cause' stands in the question and the prohibition: Job fears God, and a man is not to be contended with, for no cause; the sense is the absence of ground, which the ESV gives as 'for no reason'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 1:9 (reason), Pro 3:30 (reason)
- **Observation 22174** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Isa 1:18 the verb whose usual sense is 'to rebuke, prove' stands in the Niphal cohortative, 'let us reason together': an invitation to argue the case out between the Lord and the people, followed by what the Lord says; the sense is to set a case before one another, not to rebuke.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 1:18 (reason)
- **Observation 22175** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Sa 20:29 the adverb 'so, therefore' in the phrase the ESV gives as 'for this reason' ties Jonathan's report that David has not come to the family sacrifice and to his brother's command; it is a connective of consequence.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 20:29 (reason)
- **Observation 22176** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 41:31 and Gen 47:13 the noun 'face' is joined with a preposition and the famine in the phrase the ESV renders 'by reason of the famine': it states the cause of the plenty being unknown and of the lands languishing; it is a prepositional use, not the face.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Gen 41:31 (reason), Gen 47:13 (reason)
- **Observation 22177** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 22:24 the verb 'to think' stands in a clause about which of the disciples is to be regarded as greatest: it is the standing assigned to a person, 'to be reckoned', and not what the subject thinks.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 22:24 (regarded)
- **Observation 22178** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 2:26 and Rom 8:36 the verb 'to reckon' is passive and carries an 'as': the uncircumcised will be counted as circumcision, and the believers are counted as sheep for slaughter; the sense is 'to be counted as', a status assigned.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Rom 2:26 (regarded), Rom 8:36 (regarded)
- **Observation 22179** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 31:15, Psa 44:22, Lam 4:2 and Hos 8:12 the Niphal perfect of the verb 'to reckon' is followed by 'as' and the thing compared: foreigners, sheep to be slaughtered, earthen pots, a strange thing; the sense is 'to be accounted as', the status others give.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Gen 31:15 (regarded), Psa 44:22 (regarded), Lam 4:2 (regarded), Hos 8:12 (regarded)
- **Observation 22180** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Isa 29:16 and Isa 29:17 the Niphal imperfect of the verb 'to reckon' is in two questions with 'as' and the thing compared (the potter as clay; the fruitful field as a forest); the sense is 'to be counted as', a status turned upside down.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Isa 29:16 (regarded), Isa 29:17 (regarded)
- **Observation 22181** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 5:16 the present verb 'to know' with 'thus' no longer is the second half of the verse's contrast with the earlier regarding of Christ; the sense is to know or size up, now ended as a way of knowing him.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 5:16 (regard)
- **Observation 22182** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Th 3:15 and 1Ti 6:1 the present imperative of the verb 'to lead, count' has an 'as' complement: do not count him as an enemy, let bondservants count their masters as worthy of honor; the sense is 'to esteem as', a judgment of status.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 2Th 3:15 (regard), 1Ti 6:1 (regard)
- **Observation 22183** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 4:1 and 1Pe 5:12 the verb 'to reckon' has a person as object and an 'as' complement: in 1Cor 4:1 how the apostles are to be taken, as servants and stewards, and in 1Pe 5:12 Peter's estimate of Silvanus as a faithful brother; the sense is 'to consider as'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 1Cor 4:1 (regard), 1Pe 5:12 (regard)
- **Observation 22184** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Col 2:16 the noun 'part, share' stands in a prepositional idiom ('in a part of') that introduces the topics of food, drink, festival, new moon and Sabbath; it means 'with regard to' and is not a thinking word, which departs from the dictionary sense 'part'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Col 2:16 (regard)
- **Observation 22185** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 5:16 the perfect verb 'to know' has 'no one' as object and 'according to the flesh' as its standard; the lexicon lists 'to have regard for one, to pay attention to' for the code, and the verse sets it before the later regard for Christ.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 5:16 (regard)
- **Observation 22186** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Exo 5:9 the negative 'not' (used with a verb: let not) stands in the jussive and bears on the regarding of lying words (H8159 in the same verse): the work is to be heavier so that the men do not pay heed; the verse states its reach.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Exo 5:9 (regard)
- **Observation 22187** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 28:5 the Qal imperfect of the verb 'to discern' is negated and has the works of the Lord as object: they do not discern or give heed to them; the sense is attentive understanding, which the verse says is lacking in the wicked.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 28:5 (regard)
- **Observation 22188** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ecc 3:18 the noun 'cause, manner' stands in an idiom that opens 'I said in my heart': it means 'with regard to' the children of man and is a prepositional use, not a word of thought; it departs from the dictionary sense 'cause'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 3:18 (regard)
- **Observation 22189** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Isa 13:17 and Isa 33:8 the Qal of the verb 'to reckon' is negated and has silver and man as objects: the Medes have no regard for silver, there is no regard for man; the sense is 'to esteem, value', the lexicon's 'to esteem, value, regard'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Isa 13:17 (regard), Isa 33:8 (regard)
- **Observation 22190** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 9:21, Psa 144:3 and Pro 12:10 the verb 'to know' means to take knowing account of someone with care: the speaker does not regard himself, the Lord regards man, and the righteous regards the life of his beast; the sense is 'to take note of, acknowledge'.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Job 9:21 (regard), Psa 144:3 (regard), Pro 12:10 (regard)
- **Observation 22191** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 9:21 the negative 'not' follows 'I regard' (H3045 in the same verse) and bears on it: the verse states that he does not regard himself; the reach is the verb of regarding.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 9:21 (regard)
- **Observation 22192** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Sa 25:25 the noun 'heart' is not the speaker's word for a faculty but the object-word of an idiom of setting the heart on someone, in the plea that the lord not take Nabal to heart; the ESV renders it 'regard', and the unit is shared with the verb.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 25:25 (regard)
- **Observation 22193** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 74:20, Isa 5:12 and Lam 4:16 the Hiphil of the verb 'to look' means to look upon with regard: in Psa 74:20 the covenant is the object the Lord is asked to look to, in Isa 5:12 the feasters give no heed to what the Lord has done, in Lam 4:16 the Lord's looking on them is withdrawn; it includes both attention and favour.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Psa 74:20 (regard), Isa 5:12 (regard), Lam 4:16 (regard)
- **Observation 22194** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jer 24:5 the Hiphil of the verb 'to recognize' has the exiles as object and 'as good' as complement: the Lord will recognise and regard them as good; the sense is 'to acknowledge, regard', as the lexicon lists the Hiphil.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 24:5 (regard)
- **Observation 22195** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Ki 3:14 the verb 'to lift' is part of the idiom 'to lift the face of someone', the sense given by the lexicon head 'favour, honour': Elisha says he has regard for Jehoshaphat, which is why he acts; it is respect shown, not lifting.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Ki 3:14 (regard)
- **Observation 22196** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Sa 1:16 the verb 'to give, put' stands in a plea not to be set as a worthless woman: the speaker asks not to be reckoned as one; the verb of giving is used of reckoning, which departs from its first sense.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 1:16 (regard)
- **Observation 22197** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Lev 19:23 the verb 'to count uncircumcised' is a denominative of the foreskin: the fruit of a newly planted tree is to be counted as uncircumcised, which the verse spells out as forbidden for three years; the sense is 'to treat as foreskin, withheld'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Lev 19:23 (regard)
- **Observation 22198** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Deu 9:27 and 2Ch 6:19 the verb 'to turn' is used with a prepositional object of what the Lord is asked to turn toward or not to turn toward: do not regard the stubbornness, have regard to the prayer; it is attention directed, not a physical turning.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Deu 9:27 (regard), 2Ch 6:19 (regard)
- **Observation 22199** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 80:14 the Qal imperative 'visit' follows 'look down from heaven, and see' and has the vine as object: the sense is to attend to and care for, which the lexicon lists for the code (to attend to, look after, care for).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 80:14 (regard)
- **Observation 22200** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Deu 33:9 and Job 37:24 the verb 'to see' is negated: in Deu 33:9 the Levite's words of his parents, in Job 37:24 God's attitude to the self-wise; the sense is to look upon with regard, which the lexicon lists, here withheld.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Deu 33:9 (regard), Job 37:24 (regard)
- **Observation 22201** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 35:13 the verb 'to behold' is negated and has the empty cry as object: the Almighty does not look upon it; the sense is to take notice of, the lexicon's 'to regard'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 35:13 (regard)
- **Observation 22202** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 34:27 the Hiphil perfect of the verb 'to be prudent' is negated and has all his ways as object: they had no regard for them; the sense is 'to give heed to, consider', which the lexicon lists for the Hiphil.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 34:27 (regard)
- **Observation 22203** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Ch 5:11 the verb 'to keep, observe' is in a phrase about the priests being present without regard to their divisions; the sense is not keeping to the order of their courses, observing a rule, which the ESV gives as 'regard'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Ch 5:11 (regard)
- **Observation 22204** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 4:5, Exo 5:9 and Psa 119:117 the verb 'to gaze at' is used of regard, with its object a person's offering, lying words and the Lord's statutes: Cain's offering was not looked on, the men are not to look to lying words, the psalmist asks to look continually at the statutes.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 4:5 (regard), Exo 5:9 (regard), Psa 119:117 (regard)
- **Observation 22205** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mar 8:33 the present verb 'to think, set the mind on' is negated for Peter and set against its opposite object in the rebuke of Jesus, so that what the mind is directed to is the contrast; the sense is the directing of the thinking, as in Rom 8:5.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mar 8:33 (setting your mind)
- **Observation 22206** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 2:3 the connective 'but, and' opens the question with the verb of reckoning (G3049 in the same verse), so that the sentence runs on from the accusation of 2:1; it is read as a connective, not as a word of supposing.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 2:3 (suppose)
- **Observation 22207** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jam 4:5 the present verb 'to think' stands in a question put to the readers: do you suppose that the Scripture speaks to no purpose; the sense is 'to suppose, imagine', set in a rhetorical question.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 4:5 (suppose)
- **Observation 22208** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Joh 21:25 and Jam 1:7 the present verb 'to suppose' is set in the first person (the writer's estimate that the world could not contain the books) and in a third-person prohibition (that person must not suppose he will receive); the sense is 'to suppose, expect'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Joh 21:25 (suppose), Jam 1:7 (suppose)
- **Observation 22209** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 7:43 and Act 2:15 the verb 'to take up, suppose' is used of a considered view: Simon's answer 'I suppose' and the charge of drunkenness 'as you suppose'; the sense is 'to take it that', an assumption offered.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Luk 7:43 (suppose), Act 2:15 (suppose)
- **Observation 22210** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 13:25 the present verb 'to suppose, suspect' is in Paul's quotation of John: what do you suppose that I am; the sense is 'to suspect, take to be'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 13:25 (suppose)
- **Observation 22211** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 18:28-32 the adverb 'perhaps' is the word with which Abraham sets a conceivable number of righteous to the Lord, five times with the number falling from forty-five to ten; it introduces a case put for argument, which the ESV renders 'suppose'.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Gen 18:28 (suppose), Gen 18:29 (suppose), Gen 18:30 (suppose), Gen 18:31 (suppose), Gen 18:32 (suppose)
- **Observation 22212** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Sa 13:32 and 2Sa 13:33 the verb 'to say' stands in 'let not my lord suppose' that all the king's sons are dead: the lexicon lists 'to say in one's heart, to think' for the Qal, and the verse gives the content of what he is not to think.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 2Sa 13:32 (suppose), 2Sa 13:33 (suppose)
- **Observation 22213** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Joh 11:31, Joh 20:15 and Act 27:13 the participle of the verb 'to think' states a supposition on which the people act: the Jews supposed Mary was going to weep at the tomb, Mary supposed him the gardener, the sailors supposed they had their purpose; the sense is 'supposing'.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Joh 11:31 (supposing), Joh 20:15 (supposing), Act 27:13 (supposing)
- **Observation 22214** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 2:44, Act 14:19 and Act 16:27 the participle of the verb 'to think' states an assumption on which a step is taken: they went a day's journey, they dragged Paul out, the jailer drew his sword; the sense is 'supposing', an assumption of a state of affairs.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Luk 2:44 (supposing), Act 14:19 (supposing), Act 16:27 (supposing)
- **Observation 22215** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 1:21 the plural noun 'reasonings' stands in the dative as the sphere in which they became futile, parallel with their foolish hearts being darkened; the sense is 'thoughts, reasonings', their line of thinking.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:21 (thinking)
- **Observation 22216** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 12:19 the present verb 'to think' with 'all along' is a question to the readers: have you been supposing that we defend ourselves to you; the sense is 'to suppose'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 12:19 (thinking)
- **Observation 22217** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Pe 4:1 the noun 'thought, attitude' is the thing with which the readers are to arm themselves as Christ suffered; the lexicon lists 'thought, purpose, intention, attitude' for this verse, and here it is a settled way of thinking.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Pe 4:1 (thinking)
- **Observation 22218** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 1:21 the connective 'and' joins the becoming futile in their thinking to the darkening of their foolish hearts; it is read as a connective (RG-07) in the unit of 'thinking' and carries no thinking of its own.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:21 (thinking)
- **Observation 22219** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Heb 11:15 the imperfect verb 'to remember, think of' is conditional, with that land from which they went out as object; the sense is keeping something in mind, which the verse says would have given them opportunity to return.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Heb 11:15 (thinking)
- **Observation 22220** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Phili 1:17 the present participle 'to suppose' states the intention of those who proclaim Christ out of selfish ambition: they suppose or intend to afflict Paul in his imprisonment; the sense is 'to suppose, expect'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Phili 1:17 (thinking)
- **Observation 22221** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 14:20 the plural noun 'midriff, understanding' appears twice, in the command not to be children in it and to be mature in it; the lexicon lists 'the mind, intellect, understanding' for this verse, so it is the faculty of understanding.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 14:20 (thinking)
- **Observation 22222** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 32:8, 1Sa 27:11, 1Sa 27:12, 2Sa 5:6, 2Ki 7:12, Neh 6:9 and Psa 64:5 the verb 'to say' introduces what a person says to himself, and the verse gives the thought in direct speech; the lexicon lists 'to say in one's heart, to think', and the ESV renders it 'thinking'.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Gen 32:8 (thinking), 1Sa 27:11 (thinking), 1Sa 27:12 (thinking), 2Sa 5:6 (thinking), 2Ki 7:12 (thinking), Neh 6:9 (thinking), Psa 64:5 (thinking)
- **Observation 22223** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Ch 32:1 the same verb has no quoted thought after it: Sennacherib invades and 'thinking to win them' states what he intends; it is purpose and not a thought spoken to himself, which departs from the sense in the other seven verses.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Ch 32:1 (thinking)
- **Observation 22224** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 8:18, 1Cor 3:18, 1Cor 10:12, 1Cor 14:37, Gal 6:3, Phili 3:4 and Jam 1:26 the present verb 'to think' states what a person holds of himself or of what he has: wise, standing, a prophet, something, having reason for confidence, religious; the sense is a self-estimate, which the verse then tests.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Luk 8:18 (thinks), 1Cor 3:18 (thinks), 1Cor 10:12 (thinks), 1Cor 14:37 (thinks), Gal 6:3 (thinks), Phili 3:4 (thinks), Jam 1:26 (thinks)
- **Observation 22225** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 14:14 the participle of the verb 'to reckon' has the thing as its object and 'unclean' as complement: for anyone who reckons it unclean it is unclean; the sense is 'to hold to be', a personal judgment that the verse treats as binding on that person.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 14:14 (thinks)
- **Observation 22226** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 7:36 the present verb 'to think' states the man's judgment about his conduct toward his betrothed; the sense is 'to suppose, judge', the lexicon's 'to deem, 1Cor 7:26'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 7:36 (thinks)
- **Observation 22227** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Sa 20:3 the verb 'to say' introduces what Jonathan's father says to himself, given in direct speech: he thinks, do not let Jonathan know; the sense is 'to say in one's heart, to think'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 20:3 (thinks)
- **Observation 22228** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Pe 1:13 the connective 'now, and' stands in the unit of 'think' ahead of 'I think it right' and turns from the previous verse to the writer's resolve to remind; it is read as a connective (RG-07), not as the verb of thinking.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Pe 1:13 (think)
- **Observation 22229** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 17:25, 18:12, 21:28, 22:17, 22:42, Luk 10:36, Joh 11:56 and Heb 10:29 the present verb 'to think' stands in a question that asks the hearer for his opinion: what do you think, which of these three do you think; the sense is 'to hold an opinion', asked for.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Mat 17:25 (think), Mat 18:12 (think), Mat 21:28 (think), Mat 22:17 (think), Mat 22:42 (think), Luk 10:36 (think), Joh 11:56 (think), Heb 10:29 (think)
- **Observation 22230** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 6:7, 26:53, Luk 12:51, 13:2, 13:4, Joh 5:39, Joh 5:45 and Joh 16:2 the verb 'to think' states a supposition or assumption about a state of affairs, often one the verse corrects: that many words will be heard, that Jesus cannot appeal, that he came to give peace; the sense is 'to suppose'.
  - applies to 8 occurrence(s): Mat 6:7 (think), Mat 26:53 (think), Luk 12:51 (think), Luk 13:2 (think), Luk 13:4 (think), Joh 5:39 (think), Joh 5:45 (think), Joh 16:2 (think)
- **Observation 22231** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 4:9, 1Cor 7:40, 1Cor 12:23 and 2Cor 11:16 the verb 'to think' states the speaker's own judgment or esteem (I think God has exhibited us, we think less honorable, let no one think me foolish); the sense is 'to judge, deem', an estimate of persons or parts.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 1Cor 4:9 (think), 1Cor 7:40 (think), 1Cor 12:23 (think), 2Cor 11:16 (think)
- **Observation 22232** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 9:4 the present verb 'to reflect on' has evil things as object and the hearts as the place: Jesus asks why they entertain evil in their hearts, so the verb is a sustained inward turning over of a matter, in a verse where he first knows their thoughts (G1761, same verse).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mat 9:4 (think)
- **Observation 22233** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Pe 1:13 the present verb 'to lead, count' is in the first person with the judgment that follows (it is right to stir up by reminder); the sense is 'to consider, hold' something to be right, the lexicon's 'think, consider, count'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Pe 1:13 (think)
- **Observation 22234** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 12:6 the verb 'to reckon' has the speaker as object, set against what others see and hear of him: the worry is the estimate others form of Paul beyond the evidence; the sense is 'to credit, form an estimate of a person'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 12:6 (think)
- **Observation 22235** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Phili 4:8 the present imperative of the verb 'to reckon' has the whole list of true, honorable, just, pure and lovely things as its object: the readers are to take these into account and dwell on them; the sense is a directed reflection on named objects.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Phili 4:8 (think)
- **Observation 22236** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Eph 3:20 the verb 'to understand, perceive' is set beside asking as the two things that God does beyond; the lexicon lists 'to conceive, Eph 3:20', so the sense is to form an idea of, to conceive in the mind.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Eph 3:20 (think)
- **Observation 22237** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 5:17, Mat 10:34 and Act 17:29 the verb 'to think, suppose' is negated (do not suppose, we ought not to suppose) and names a false assumption the verse then corrects: about Jesus' coming, and about the divine being; the sense is 'to suppose, assume'.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mat 5:17 (think), Mat 10:34 (think), Act 17:29 (think)
- **Observation 22238** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 7:26 the present verb 'to think' states Paul's own judgment about what is good in the present distress; the lexicon lists 'to deem, 1Cor 7:26' for the code.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 7:26 (think)
- **Observation 22239** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rom 12:3 the verb 'to be minded' occurs in an infinitive series that sets thinking of oneself too highly against thinking with sober judgment; the sense is the measure and direction of the mind's estimate of oneself, the attitude held.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 12:3 (think)
- **Observation 22240** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Phili 3:15 the verb 'to be minded' is in a subjunctive and an indicative in the same sentence: the mature are to be minded in one way, and where the readers are minded otherwise God will reveal it; the sense is the mind-set held.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Phili 3:15 (think)
- **Observation 22241** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Ki 18:20, Isa 36:5 and Eze 11:5 the verb 'to say' stands for what a person holds or claims: the Assyrian's question whether words are strategy, and the Lord's naming of what Israel says in its mind; the sense is 'to say in one's heart, to think', the lexicon's Qal sense.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): 2Ki 18:20 (think), Isa 36:5 (think), Eze 11:5 (think)
- **Observation 22242** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Ch 13:8 the verb 'to say' is a participle in the charge that Israel now means to withstand the kingdom: it states a purpose they hold, not a thought spoken to themselves, which departs from the sense in the other three verses.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Ch 13:8 (think)
- **Observation 22243** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Est 4:13 the Piel verb 'to liken, imagine' is negated and followed by 'to yourself': Mordecai tells Esther not to imagine that she will escape; the lexicon lists 'to imagine, think' for the Piel, and the verse makes it a thought she is told not to hold.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Est 4:13 (think)
- **Observation 22244** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Isa 10:7 the Qal imperfect of the verb 'to plan' has the heart as subject and is negated, set against what is in his heart (to destroy); the sense is 'to devise, plan', the planning of the Assyrian that the Lord says is not what is in the king's own thought.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 10:7 (think)
- **Observation 22245** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 35:2 and Job 41:32 the verb 'to reckon' states a judgment held of a matter: do you hold this to be just, one would hold the deep to be white-haired; the sense is 'to deem, hold to be'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 35:2 (think), Job 41:32 (think)
- **Observation 22246** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 119:59 and Psa 144:3 the Piel and the wayyiqtol of the verb 'to reckon' state a reflection or taking account: the psalmist reflects on his ways and turns his feet, and the psalmist asks what man is that the Lord takes account of him; the sense is 'to reflect on, take account of'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 119:59 (think), Psa 144:3 (think)
- **Observation 22247** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 6:26 and Jer 23:27 the verb 'to reckon' states an intention or design: Job asks whether they mean to reprove words, and the Lord names the prophets who plan to make the people forget his name; the sense is 'to plan, intend'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Job 6:26 (think), Jer 23:27 (think)
- **Observation 22248** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 7:25 the Aramaic verb 'to think, intend' is joined with changing the times and the law: it states what the horn plans after speaking against the Most High; the sense is 'to intend', a purpose that the verse then says is given into his hand.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 7:25 (think)
- **Observation 22249** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Num 36:6, 2Sa 10:3, 1Ch 19:3 and Jer 40:5 the noun 'eye' stands in the idiom 'in the eyes of' and the ESV gives it as 'think': whom they think best, do you think David is honoring, wherever you think right; the eye is the standing point of a judgment, so the sense is 'in the view of', not the organ of sight.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Num 36:6 (think), 2Sa 10:3 (think), 1Ch 19:3 (think), Jer 40:5 (think)
- **Observation 22250** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Sa 18:27 the Qal participle of the verb 'to see' is the watchman's judgment of what he sees of the running: a conclusion formed from observation; the lexicon lists 'to perceive, consider' for the Qal.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Sa 18:27 (think)
- **Observation 22251** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 15:19 and Mar 7:21 the plural noun 'reasonings' is the first item in lists of what comes out of the heart, before the sins named after it; the sense is the reasonings that issue from the heart as a source.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 15:19 (thoughts), Mar 7:21 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22252** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 2:35, Luk 5:22, Luk 6:8 and 1Cor 3:20 the plural noun 'reasonings' is what is known or revealed to another: Jesus perceives and knows what they reason, what lies in many hearts is revealed, and the Lord knows the reasonings of the wise and finds them futile; the sense is the inner line of thought, open to the one who knows it.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Luk 2:35 (thoughts), Luk 5:22 (thoughts), Luk 6:8 (thoughts), 1Cor 3:20 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22253** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jam 2:4 the plural noun 'reasonings' is in the genitive of quality, 'judges of evil reasonings': it is the thinking by which the readers make distinctions among themselves.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 2:4 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22254** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 11:17 the noun 'thought' is what Jesus knows before he answers; the lexicon lists the word for this verse alone, and the sense is an inward thought known to him.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 11:17 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22255** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 1:51 the noun 'mind, thinking' is in the dative with the hearts: the proud are scattered in the thinking of their hearts; the lexicon gives the part of the inner person that thinks, whose seat is the heart.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 1:51 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22256** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 9:4, Mat 12:25 and Heb 4:12 the plural noun 'thought' is the inner content of persons that another sees: Jesus knows their thoughts, and the word of God discerns the thoughts and intentions of the heart; the sense is 'thought', set with the heart as the place.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Mat 9:4 (thoughts), Mat 12:25 (thoughts), Heb 4:12 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22257** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 11:3 the plural noun 'minds, thoughts' is the subject of being led astray from devotion to Christ; the lexicon lists 'the heart, soul, disposition' and 'a conception of the mind' for this code, and here it is the readers' thinking.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 11:3 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22258** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 2:11 the stored word is the article ahead of the genitive 'of the man', which the ESV renders as 'thoughts'; it stands for 'the things of' the person and is not a word of thought by itself, and its morph is empty in the stored data, which is flagged as a data error (RG-16), not corrected in the reading.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 2:11 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22259** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Eze 38:10 the plural noun 'words, matters' is what comes up on the heart of Gog in the verse: matters arising in his mind, which lead to the evil scheme; it is the noun for things, used here for what arises in the mind.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Eze 38:10 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22260** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Lam 3:62 the noun 'meditation, musing' (construct) stands beside the lips of the assailants and is against the speaker all day; the lexicon lists 'plotting' for the word, so it is the muttered musing of hostile people.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Lam 3:62 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22261** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ecc 10:20 the noun 'knowledge, mind' with the preposition marks the place of the thought: even in the mind, the king is not to be cursed, set beside the bedroom; the sense is 'mind, the place of thinking' as the lexicon lists.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 10:20 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22262** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 10:4 the plural noun 'devices, purposes' is what the wicked holds with the pride of his face: all his purposes are that there is no God; the lexicon lists 'purpose, device', and the verse gives the content as a denial of God.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 10:4 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22263** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 6:5, Job 21:27, Psa 56:5, Pro 15:26, Isa 55:7, Isa 59:7 (two occurrences) and Jer 4:14 the plural noun 'thoughts, devices' is what the wicked hold and plan: evil continually, schemes to wrong, thoughts against the speaker, an abomination to the Lord, to be forsaken, thoughts of iniquity, wicked thoughts that lodge; the sense is the planned or entertained thoughts of persons, evil in each.
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Gen 6:5 (thoughts), Job 21:27 (thoughts), Psa 56:5 (thoughts), Pro 15:26 (thoughts), Isa 55:7 (thoughts), Isa 59:7 (thoughts), Jer 4:14 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22264** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Ch 29:18, Psa 94:11, Pro 12:5 and Isa 66:18 the plural noun 'thoughts' is the thinking of persons as open to God and having a moral quality: kept toward the Lord, known as a breath, just in the righteous, known with their works; the sense is thoughts as the content of the mind that God knows.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): 1Ch 29:18 (thoughts), Psa 94:11 (thoughts), Pro 12:5 (thoughts), Isa 66:18 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22265** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 40:5, Psa 92:5, Mic 4:12 and Isa 55:8-9 the plural noun 'thoughts' is the Lord's own thinking: his thoughts toward us, very deep, not known by the nations, and in Isa 55:8-9 set against the thoughts of those addressed as higher; the sense is God's purposes and thinking.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Psa 40:5 (thoughts), Psa 92:5 (thoughts), Isa 55:8 (thoughts), Isa 55:9 (thoughts), Mic 4:12 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22266** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 20:2 the plural noun 'disquietings' with the possessive is the subject: his thoughts answer him because of his haste; it is a troubled, restless reflection, a word that the lexicon gives only as disquietings.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 20:2 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22267** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 139:2 the noun 'thought, purpose' (singular, with the speaker's suffix) is what the Lord discerns from afar, beside his sitting and rising; the sense is the speaker's inner thought, known to God.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 139:2 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22268** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 139:17 the plural noun 'thoughts' is God's own, called precious and too vast to be counted; the sense is the thinking of God, set against the speaker's thought of 139:2.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 139:17 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22269** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 2:29 and Dan 2:30 the Aramaic noun 'thought' is what came to the king on his bed concerning what would be, and the thoughts of his mind that Daniel is to make known; the sense is thoughts of the mind, shown to Daniel by revelation.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Dan 2:29 (thoughts), Dan 2:30 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22270** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Dan 4:19, Dan 5:6, Dan 5:10 and Dan 7:28 the Aramaic noun 'thoughts' is the subject of alarming the person: Daniel dismayed, the king's color changed, the queen's counsel, Daniel's color changed; the sense is troubling thoughts that disturb.
  - applies to 4 occurrence(s): Dan 4:19 (thoughts), Dan 5:6 (thoughts), Dan 5:10 (thoughts), Dan 7:28 (thoughts)
- **Observation 22271** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 12:17 the imperfect verb 'to reason through' is followed by 'to himself' and the question of what to do with the crops: the man deliberates inwardly; the lexicon lists 'to reason, deliberate, ponder', and the unit also holds the verb 'to say' (G3004G).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 12:17 (thought)
- **Observation 22272** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mar 6:49, Luk 24:37, Joh 11:13, Joh 13:29 and Act 12:9 the verb 'to think' states a supposition formed from what is seen or heard: a ghost, a spirit, rest in sleep, a purchase, a vision; in each the verse shows it to be a mistaken or partial supposition.
  - applies to 5 occurrence(s): Mar 6:49 (thought), Luk 24:37 (thought), Joh 11:13 (thought), Joh 13:29 (thought), Act 12:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22273** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 9:5 and Phili 2:25 the verb 'to lead, count' states a considered judgment that something is necessary: urging the brothers to go ahead, sending Epaphroditus; the sense is 'to judge, deem'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 2Cor 9:5 (thought), Phili 2:25 (thought)
- **Observation 22274** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Act 26:8 the passive verb 'to judge' has 'incredible' as complement: the question is whether it is judged incredible that God raises the dead; the sense is 'to be judged, reckoned to be', an estimate held by the hearers.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 26:8 (thought)
- **Observation 22275** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Luk 12:17 the participle of the verb 'to say' is the second word of the unit 'thought' (the verb G1260 beside it): the man speaks within himself, so the saying is inner speech, which departs from the verb's usual sense of spoken words.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 12:17 (thought)
- **Observation 22276** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Ti 3:7 the noun 'testimony, reputation' is the thing the overseer must have from outsiders; the lexicon lists the reputation sense, and the ESV renders it as being well thought of: it is the estimate outsiders hold of him.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ti 3:7 (thought)
- **Observation 22277** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Cor 10:5 the noun 'thought' is the object of the taking captive to obey Christ, set beside arguments and lofty opinions; the sense is the product of the mind, every one of them.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 10:5 (thought)
- **Observation 22278** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mat 20:10 and Act 8:20 the verb 'to think, suppose' states an expectation or assumption on which the person acts: the first hired expected more, Simon assumed he could buy the gift; the sense is 'to suppose, expect'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Mat 20:10 (thought), Act 8:20 (thought)
- **Observation 22279** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 13:11 the imperfect verb 'to be minded' stands between speaking and reasoning as a way of working things out in childhood; the sense is a mode of thinking, the mind-set of a child.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 13:11 (thought)
- **Observation 22280** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jon 1:6 the adverb 'perhaps' opens the captain's hope that the god will take notice of them (the verb of giving thought, H6245B, is in the same verse); it marks a possibility, not a thought of its own.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jon 1:6 (thought)
- **Observation 22281** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 20:11, 26:9, 32:20, Exo 2:14, Jos 22:28, Jdg 3:24, 1Sa 16:6, 18:11, 18:17, 18:21, 20:26, 2Sa 14:15, 14:17, 2Ki 5:11, 20:19, Job 29:18, Psa 94:18, Isa 39:8, Jer 3:7 and Eze 20:32 the verb 'to say' introduces what a person says to himself and the verse gives the thought as quoted speech; the sense is 'to say in one's heart, to think'.
  - applies to 20 occurrence(s): Gen 20:11 (thought), Gen 26:9 (thought), Gen 32:20 (thought), Exo 2:14 (thought), Jos 22:28 (thought), Judg 3:24 (thought), 1Sa 16:6 (thought), 1Sa 18:11 (thought), 1Sa 18:17 (thought), 1Sa 18:21 (thought), 1Sa 20:26 (thought), 2Sa 14:15 (thought); and 8 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 22282** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 31:31, Jdg 15:2 and Jer 3:19 the verb 'to say' introduces an assumption or expectation in an indirect clause: that Laban would take his daughters, that she utterly hated her, that you would call me Father; the sense is 'to think, assume' with the thought stated as what he took to be so.
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Gen 31:31 (thought), Judg 15:2 (thought), Jer 3:19 (thought)
- **Observation 22283** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Rut 4:4 and 2Sa 21:16 the verb 'to say' states a purpose: to tell the kinsman of the matter, and to kill David; it is an intention held, which departs from the sense of a belief or assumption in the other verses.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Rut 4:4 (thought), 2Sa 21:16 (thought)
- **Observation 22284** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Pro 14:15 and Pro 21:29 the Qal imperfect of the verb 'to discern' has the steps and the ways as object and sets the prudent and the upright against the simple and the wicked; the sense is 'to give careful thought to, attend to', which the lexicon lists as 'to give heed to'.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 14:15 (thought), Pro 21:29 (thought)
- **Observation 22285** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Deu 15:9 the noun 'word, matter' is the thing that might be present in the heart, an unworthy matter that leads to the grudging look and giving nothing; the noun for a word is used of what is held inwardly, which departs from its sense of speech.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22286** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Num 33:56 the Piel perfect of the verb 'to liken, imagine' states what the Lord purposed to do to the Canaanites and will now do to Israel; the sense is 'to purpose, devise', a plan held.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Num 33:56 (thought)
- **Observation 22287** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 48:9 the Piel perfect of the verb 'to imagine, think' has the steadfast love as object and the temple as place: the worshippers have reflected on it; the sense is 'to ponder, think on'.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 48:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22288** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 50:21 the Piel perfect of the verb 'to imagine' states the false supposition of the wicked about God, answered by the Lord's rebuke; the sense is 'to suppose, imagine', which the lexicon lists for the Piel.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 50:21 (thought)
- **Observation 22289** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Deu 15:9 the verb 'to be' (jussive) is the 'lest there be' of the clause, joined in the unit 'thought' with the noun for the matter (H1697G, same verse); it is the existential copula and not a word of thought.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Deu 15:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22290** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Lam 1:9 the Qal perfect of the verb 'to remember' is negated: she did not call her future to mind, which the verse sets before her fall; the sense is 'to remember, take thought of', withheld.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Lam 1:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22291** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Sa 18:25 the Qal perfect of the verb 'to plan' states Saul's purpose in the bride-price he sets: to bring about David's fall; the sense is 'to devise, plan' with a harmful end.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:25 (thought)
- **Observation 22292** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 40:17 the Qal imperfect of the verb 'to plan' has the Lord as subject and the poor and needy speaker as the one he thinks on; the sense is 'to take thought for', the planning of care.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 40:17 (thought)
- **Observation 22293** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Gen 38:15 the wayyiqtol of the verb 'to reckon' states the conclusion Judah drew from her covered face: he took her for a prostitute; the sense is 'to take to be', a judgment formed from appearance.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gen 38:15 (thought)
- **Observation 22294** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Psa 73:16 the Piel wayyiqtol of the verb 'to reckon' states the speaker's attempt to work out the prosperity of the wicked, which he found wearisome; the sense is 'to set the mind to understand', an effortful working out.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 73:16 (thought)
- **Observation 22295** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ecc 9:10 the noun 'account, reasoning' is in a list with work, knowledge and wisdom that are not in Sheol; the lexicon gives 'account, reasoning, reckoning', and the sense is the activity of reckoning.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 9:10 (thought)
- **Observation 22296** (lexical, tag term, marker evidence, status draft): In 1Ch 28:9 the noun 'thought, device' is paired with plan as what the Lord understands when he searches all hearts; the sense is the thought or device of a heart, known to him.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ch 28:9 (thought)
- **Observation 22297** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 2Sa 3:19 the noun 'eye' is in the idiom 'in the eyes of' with Israel and Benjamin: what seemed good in their view; the ESV renders it 'thought good', and the eye is the standpoint of the judgment, not the organ of sight.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Sa 3:19 (thought)
- **Observation 22298** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Jon 1:6 the Hithpael verb 'to think' states the hope that the god will take thought of the sailors so that they do not perish; the sense is 'to give thought, take notice', and the verse sets it after perhaps (H0194).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jon 1:6 (thought)
- **Observation 22299** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Job 12:5 the noun 'thought' in the construct stands for the attitude of the one at ease toward misfortune: contempt for it; the lexicon gives 'thought, idea', and the sense is the opinion of the comfortable.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 12:5 (thought)
- **Observation 22300** (lexical, tag face, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Mic 2:8 the Qal participle of the verb 'to return' in the construct with war is the description of the trusting passersby: those who come back from war; the ESV gives it as 'no thought of war', but the stored word is the returning, not a word of thought.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mic 2:8 (thought)
- **Observation 22301** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Amo 4:13 the noun 'thought, musing' with the suffix is what the Lord declares to man; the sense is a person's own thought, laid open by the one who made the mountains.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Amo 4:13 (thought)
- **Observation 22302** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ecc 12:9 the Piel perfect of the verb 'to weigh' is the first of three acts of the Preacher in handling proverbs (weighing, studying, arranging); the lexicon lists 'weigh, test, consider', and here it is a careful judging of each proverb before it is set in order.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 12:9 (weighing)
- **Observation 22303** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In 1Cor 14:29 the present imperative of the verb 'to distinguish, judge' is given to the others when two or three prophets have spoken: they are to evaluate what is said; the sense is 'to discern, judge' between the sayings.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 14:29 (weigh)
- **Observation 22304** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Zep 1:11 the adjective 'laden' (plural construct) describes the traders as those loaded with silver; the ESV renders it as weighing out silver, but the stored word is the adjective of being laden, not a verb of weighing.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Zep 1:11 (weigh)
- **Observation 22305** (lexical, tag term, marker not yet marked, status draft): In Ezr 8:29 the Qal imperfect of the verb 'to weigh' is the act of weighing the silver and the vessels before the priests, Levites and heads of fathers' houses; the sense is 'to weigh out' for an accounting, a physical weighing and not a mental one.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ezr 8:29 (weigh)
- **Observation 12647** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Who holds it, and toward whom: A people "a horror" to those who pass by and hiss; the prophet: "dismay has taken hold on me"; Eli's "heart trembled for the ark of God"; "I am afraid that … your thoughts will be led astray"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 11:3 (thoughts)
- **Observation 13008** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): What the angry person does with it: Nehemiah: "I took counsel with myself"; Absalom "spoke to Amnon neither good nor bad"; Jacob's sons answered "deceitfully"; Samuel "cried to the Lord all night"; David "was afraid of the Lord that day"; Sanballat and Tobiah "plotted together"; Ahab "lay down on his bed and turned away his face"; Paul "reasoned in the synagogue".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 13043** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): A person's anger leads to counsel, then open charge; reasoning: "I took counsel with myself, and I brought charges against the nobles"; "So he reasoned in the synagogue".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 13673** (prior-capture, tag link, marker not yet marked, status draft): his spirit provoked as he saw idols → so he reasoned (Act 17:16–17); many days → greatly annoyed → commanded the spirit → it came out (16:18).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 14208** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): How it co-exists: With enforced silence first. "Elihu had waited to speak to Job because they were older than he" (32:4), and "I was timid and afraid to declare my opinion" (32:6). Fear and deference hold the anger in, until the others fall silent; Elihu describes the pressure in bodily terms: "I am full of words; the spirit within me constrains me. Behold, my belly is like wine that has no vent; like new wineskins ready to burst. I must speak, that I may find relief" (32:18–20). The held anger is felt as pressure in the belly, and speech is its release.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 32:6 (opinion)
- **Observation 14217** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Part 2 verses: what they add. The one wronged is now God, or God's law, rather than a sister, a friend or the poor: The indignation seizes its bearer: "Hot indignation seizes me because of the wicked, who forsake your law" (Psa 119:53). The bearer is its object as well as its subject. It is framed between comfort in God's rules (119:52) and "Your statutes have been my songs" (119:54). The indignation sits inside a life held by the law it defends; The spirit provoked by what it sees: "while Paul was waiting … his spirit was provoked within him as he saw that the city was full of idols. So he reasoned in the synagogue … and in the marketplace every day" (Act 17:16–17). This is the same movemen ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Act 17:17 (reasoned)
- **Observation 14279** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Where it comes from. Every verse, or the one next to it, names a source, and the sources are inner: Passions at war within: "What causes quarrels and what causes fights among you? Is it not this, that your passions are at war within you? You desire and do not have, so you murder. You covet and cannot obtain, so you fight and quarrel" (Jam 4:1–2). The outer fight is the inner war carried outward; The flesh: "for you are still of the flesh. For while there is jealousy and strife among you, are you not of the flesh and behaving only in a human way?" (1Cor 3:3), after "I could not address you as spiritual people, but as people of the flesh" (3:1); Conceit: "Let us not become conceited, provoking ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Ti 6:5 (imagining)
- **Observation 14664** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Where it comes from: the agent's own heart: Assyria: "But he does not so intend, and his heart does not so think; but it is in his heart to destroy, and to cut off nations not a few; for he says: 'Are not my commanders all kings?'" (Isa 10:7–8); Babylon: "I was angry with my people … I gave them into your hand; you showed them no mercy; on the aged you made your yoke exceedingly heavy. You said, 'I shall be mistress forever,' so that you did not lay these things to heart or remember their end" (47:6–7). And "who say in your heart, 'I am, and there is no one besides me'" (47:8).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 10:7 (think)
- **Observation 14918** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): (v2) Isa 10:5 (UA): Context seat: Isa 10:7 "his heart does not so think … in his heart to destroy"; Whose · check: Assyria's · genuine; Already read in: F v4 §12.1; C v3 §2.1; §: §37.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Isa 10:7 (think)
- **Observation 15049** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): "For although they knew God, they did not honor him as God or give thanks to him, but they became futile in their thinking, and their foolish hearts were darkened" (1:21). They "exchanged the glory of the immortal God for images" (1:23). Then three times "God gave them up" (1:24, 26, 28), each after an exchange (1:23, 25, and "did not see fit to acknowledge God", 1:28).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:21 (thinking)
- **Observation 15117** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): It has no room for God: "In the pride of his face the wicked does not seek him; all his thoughts are, 'There is no God'" (Psa 10:4); "his soul is puffed up; it is not upright within him, but the righteous shall live by his faith" (Hab 2:4). Then: "an arrogant man who is never at rest. His greed is as wide as Sheol" (2:5). The swollen soul is set against faith, and joined to restlessness and greed.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 10:4 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15122** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Psalm 119 gives one person's whole experience of the insolent: "The insolent utterly deride me, but I do not turn away from your law" (119:51); "The insolent smear me with lies, but with my whole heart I keep your precepts; their heart is unfeeling like fat" (119:69–70); "they have wronged me with falsehood; as for me, I will meditate on your precepts" (119:78); "The insolent have dug pitfalls for me; they do not live according to your law" (119:85); "let not the insolent oppress me" (119:122); The insolent wander from the commandments (119:21). Against them each time, the speaker holds the law, meditates, and asks for a pledge.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 119:78 (meditate)
- **Observation 15151** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The Greek picture is swelling, physioō, "to inflate". In Paul it is set again and again against love: Knowledge. "This 'knowledge' puffs up, but love builds up. If anyone imagines that he knows something, he does not yet know as he ought to know. But if anyone loves God, he is known by God" (1Co 8:1–3). Swelling knowledge is answered not by more knowledge but by being known by God; Love is not. "Love is patient and kind; love does not envy or boast; it is not arrogant" (1Co 13:4). Before this: "if I have prophetic powers, and understand all mysteries and all knowledge ... but have not love, I am nothing" (13:2); Party; "that none of you may be puffed up in favor of one against another. For w ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Cor 8:2 (imagines)
- **Observation 15152** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The measure assigned. "not to think of himself more highly than he ought to think, but to think with sober judgment, each according to the measure of faith that God has assigned" (Rom 12:3). The right measure of oneself is what God assigned. It opens with "by the grace given to me".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 12:3 (think)
- **Observation 15154** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Lofty opinions. "every lofty opinion raised against the knowledge of God, and take every thought captive to obey Christ" (2Co 10:5). Height here is a thought raised against knowing God.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Cor 10:5 (thought)
- **Observation 15158** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): From within. "from within, out of the heart of man, come evil thoughts ... coveting, wickedness, deceit, sensuality, envy, slander, pride, foolishness. All these evil things come from within, and they defile a person" (Mar 7:21–23). Jesus places pride in the heart.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Mar 7:21 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15165** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Scattered in their thoughts. "He has shown strength with his arm; he has scattered the proud in the thoughts of their hearts; he has brought down the mighty from their thrones and exalted those of humble estate" (Luk 1:51–52). The pride is located "in the thoughts of their hearts", and that is where it is scattered.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Luk 1:51 (thoughts)
- **Observation 15170** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): The given-or-taken line is stated outright. "What do you have that you did not receive? If then you received it, why do you boast as if you did not receive it?" (1Co 4:7): This is the plainest statement in the three batches of what the researcher suspected. Human pride holds as its own what was received; The researcher's word "assumed" fits: the standing is taken up as if it were not given; The right measure is the one God assigns. "not to think of himself more highly than he ought ... according to the measure of faith that God has assigned" (Rom 12:3). I read: where God has height by nature, a person has a measure by gift. Pride is thinking past the gift; Taking God's seat; "takes his seat ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Luk 1:51 (thoughts), Rom 12:3 (think)
- **Observation 15192** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Not by comparing. "if anyone thinks he is something, when he is nothing, he deceives himself. But let each one test his own work, and then his reason to boast will be in himself alone and not in his neighbor" (Gal 6:3–4). Thinking oneself something when one is nothing is named self-deception.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gal 6:3 (thinks)
- **Observation 15196** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Corinthians 10–12 is one long passage in which Paul is forced to boast, and he turns the boast round: As a fool. "accept me as a fool, so that I too may boast a little. What I am saying with this boastful confidence, I say not as the Lord would but as a fool" (2Co 11:16–17). He names the boast foolish while he makes it; Weakness. "If I must boast, I will boast of the things that show my weakness" (11:30). What follows is being let down a wall in a basket (11:32–33); Visions. "I must go on boasting. Though there is nothing to be gained by it, I will go on to visions and revelations" (12:1). Then "On behalf of this man I will boast, but on my own behalf I will not boast, except of my weaknesse ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): 2Cor 11:16 (think), 2Cor 12:6 (think)
- **Observation 15199** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): God is not the subject of a boast in these verses. He is its right object, and his character is what is boasted in. "let him who boasts boast in this, that he understands and knows me, that I am the Lord who practices steadfast love, justice, and righteousness in the earth. For in these things I delight" (Jer 9:24). The proper boast is knowing God as he is: The boast is moved, not removed. The same impulse that boasts in wisdom, might and riches (Jer 9:23) is turned to God (9:24; 1Co 1:31). It becomes joy in hope and in suffering (Rom 5:2–3); I read: set beside batches 1–3, the human "lifting" is not abolished. Where it rests on oneself it is pride. Where it rests on God it is glory in him. ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Gal 6:3 (thinks)
- **Observation 15258** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): God's height is his own, shown in acts, and not overturned: "robed in majesty ... you are from everlasting" (Psa 93:1–2). "In the greatness of your majesty you overthrow your adversaries" (Exo 15:7); Majesty goes with what only God does: abasing every proud one and saving by his own hand (Job 40:10–14); "God is not mocked" (Gal 6:7); Human pride, in the verses' own words, is a standing taken or assumed; "you make your heart like the heart of a god" (Eze 28:2); "takes his seat in the temple of God, proclaiming himself to be God" (2Th 2:4); "By the strength of my hand I have done it" (Isa 10:13); "if anyone thinks he is something, when he is nothing, he deceives himself" (Gal 6:3); Mostly, pri ... - **Observation 20491** (prior-capture, tag statement, marker not yet marked, status draft): Where responsibility sits. The commands address the stages from lodging onward: "forsake … his thoughts" (Isa 55:7); "let none of you devise evil" (Zech 7:10); "take every thought captive" (2 Cor 10:5); "wash your heart" (Jer 4:14); The *arising* is not commanded, but it is not neutral either. What comes up shows the condition of the heart (Gen 6:5; Mark 7:21). So thoughts that arise reveal the heart, and devising engages the will. The heart itself can be changed from outside: written on, given discretion, guarded, or darkened.

- **Observation 22669** (reading, tag statement, marker evidence, status draft): God is the one who discerns, knows or understands thoughts in Ps 139:2 ('you discern my thoughts'), Ps 94:11 and 1Cor 3:20 ('knows the thoughts'), Isa 66:18 ('I know their works and their thoughts') and 1Chr 28:9, where the Lord 'searches all hearts' and 'understands every plan and thought'. - **Observation 12505** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, marker not yet marked, status resolved): People value and regard: "Are we not regarded by him as foreigners?" (Gen 31:15); "Let not my lord hold me guilty" (2Sa 19:19); "who have no regard for silver" (Isa 13:17); "there is no regard for man" (Isa 33:8); "they were considered reliable" (Neh 13:13); God's laws "would be regarded as a strange thing" (Hos 8:12); "The precious sons of Zion" "how they are regarded as earthen pots" (Lam 4:2); "will be counted as cursing" (Pro 27:14). And a person is regarded: "we are regarded as sheep to be slaughtered" (Psa 44:22); "I am counted among those who go down to the pit" (Psa 88:4); Israel "not counting itself among the nations" (Num 23:9).
  - meaning: The word names an inner act of valuing a person or thing now, rightly or wrongly, and the experience of being so valued.
  - implication: Valuing is held apart from looking ahead, as the scan found (#1986, count verses).
  - applies to 7 occurrence(s): Gen 31:15 (regarded), Neh 13:13 (considered), Psa 44:22 (regarded), Isa 13:17 (regard), Isa 33:8 (regard), Lam 4:2 (regarded), Hos 8:12 (regarded)
- **Observation 12514** (strand-reading, tag key-statement, marker not yet marked, status resolved): The looking-ahead words are used most often against another person. It is placed in the heart: "let none of you devise evil against another in your heart" (Zec 7:10), "do not devise evil in your hearts against one another" (Zec 8:17). It is done together: "Come, let us make plots against Jeremiah" (Jer 18:18); "In Heshbon they planned disaster against her" (Jer 48:2); "they made plans to put him to death" (Joh 11:53), and Lazarus as well (Joh 12:10); "The soldiers’ plan was to kill the prisoners" (Act 27:42). It is aimed at the righteous and the weak: "The wicked plots against the righteous and gnashes his teeth at him" (Psa 37:12); "who have planned to trip up my feet" (Psa 140:4). It hides ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)
  - meaning: Looking ahead in these verses is mostly turned against another: devised in the heart, done together, aimed at the righteous and the weak, covered by a friendly word, and met by the plea of another.
  - implication: The plotter's power and the outcome are not the same: "though they devise mischief, they will not succeed" (Psa 21:11); "do not further their evil plot" (Psa 140:8).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 1Sa 18:25 (thought)

## 5. Claims and the narrative

42 claims rest on these occurrences: 14 on an observation of these words and 28 reached only because one of their sources is a verse where a member word stands. The second group is about its verse, not about the collection; it is kept so that nothing stored is hidden, and labelled.

### 5.1 Claims resting on an observation of these words (14)

- **C-356** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The power to look ahead is turned most often against another person: planned in the heart, done together, aimed at the righteous and the weak, hidden behind a friendly word, pinned on the other side, and met by the plea of another.
  - beyond the text: gathers the plotting verses into one account of what passes between the plotter and the plotted against
  - rests on: observation 12514 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Sa 18:25)
  - told in: chapter 10.10 Relating to others, Plotting against another; chapter 10.11 Hiding and disclosing, The hidden trap
- **C-361** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): God also thinks and plans: his plan stands where human plans cross it, he takes thought for the one in need, and he speaks in his heart.
  - beyond the text: sets God's thinking beside human thinking as the same words with a different end
  - rests on: observation 12430 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Isa 55:8, Isa 55:9, Num 33:56, Psa 40:17, Psa 40:5, Psa 92:5); observation 12423 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 6:5)
  - told in: chapter 11 Other sides of the inner being, 8. God has a heart and a soul; chapter 10.4 Thinking, Small, passing, and wholly seen; chapter 13.1 Divine, Purposing
- **C-362** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): What the heart devises on its own can be a rival worship, a false report, or a god its maker trusts.
  - beyond the text: joins the self-devised feast, the invented report and the idol as the same capacity making up what is not there
  - rests on: observation 12449 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Act 17:29)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning; chapter 12 When the inner being goes wrong, Idols taken inside; chapter 10.4 Thinking, When thinking changes character
- **C-363** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thoughts and plans are wholly open to God, the inclination is known before it acts, and the purposes of the heart will be disclosed.
  - beyond the text: reads God's knowing of the inclination as before the act, from Deu 31:21
  - rests on: observation 12431 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 28:9, Heb 4:12, Isa 66:18, Luk 11:17, Luk 5:22, Luk 6:8); observation 12413 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 28:9)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Small, passing, and wholly seen; chapter 04 The heart, The heart is hidden, and God sees it; chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning
- **C-364** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): A purpose can be set ahead and held, for one's own speech and faithfulness; it can be kept and directed by God, and the mind's setting decides its direction.
  - beyond the text: reads David's prayer as asking God to hold the purpose in place
  - rests on: observation 12434 (strand-reading key-statement, live; 1Ch 29:18)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning
- **C-366** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thoughts often arise unbidden, at night, and what arises is shaped from outside: by God's gift of skill, by instruction, or by blinding.
  - beyond the text: reads the arising thought as not chosen, from where the verses place it
  - rests on: observation 12411 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Rom 1:21); observation 12408 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Dan 2:29, Dan 2:30)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Where thoughts come from
- **C-367** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The heart is where thoughts and plans come from.
  - beyond the text: names the heart as the place of planning, from the verses that say it
  - rests on: observation 12407 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Isa 10:7, Luk 9:47, Mar 7:21, Mat 15:19)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Where thoughts come from; chapter 04 The heart, The heart thinks, attends and remembers
- **C-368** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The bed holds both deliberate plotting and meditation on God, and what fills the heart in the night shows by morning.
  - beyond the text: sets plotting and meditating on the bed side by side
  - rests on: observation 12409 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Psa 63:6)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, The night and the bed
- **C-369** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): The inclination of the heart (yetser) is the same word as the potter's thing formed and as our frame; it is evil in Gen 6:5 and stayed on God in Isa 26:3.
  - beyond the text: reads the inclination as a shaped thing, from the word's other uses

  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, The inclination: a shaped thing; chapter 12 When the inner being goes wrong, The heart as the source
- **C-370** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Read together, the verses trace a path from a thought arising to the deed: lodging, reckoning, devising, the tongue, lips and eyes, then the hand and feet; no single verse lists it.
  - beyond the text: the order is a reading of many verses together
  - rests on: observation 12424 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Dan 2:29, Isa 59:7, Jer 4:14, Psa 41:7)

  - told in: chapter 10.0 The inner being at work, Where I am answerable
- **C-372** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thinking reckons worth: people reckon wrongly, being reckoned is felt from inside, God reckons truly and counts righteousness or guilt to a person, and reckoning serves release or is laid aside in trust.
  - beyond the text: holds valuing apart from looking ahead, as the verses do
  - rests on: observation 12505 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 31:15, Hos 8:12, Isa 13:17, Isa 33:8, Lam 4:2, Neh 13:13); observation 12415 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Gen 38:15)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does
- **C-373** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thinking likens: the Hebrew damah means both to be like and to think.
  - beyond the text: reads likening as a kind of thinking, from the one verb
  - rests on: observation 12416 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Est 4:13, Isa 10:7, Num 33:56, Psa 48:9)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does
- **C-374** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Inner reasoning becomes an outward quarrel when it is shared and set on one's own standing.
  - beyond the text: reads the one Greek word for reasoning and quarrel as a turn of the same act
  - rests on: observation 12447 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Luk 9:47)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, When thinking changes character

### 5.2 Claims reached by a verse only (28)

Each claim names the verse that reached it and the member word in that verse. Read it as a claim about the verse.

| claim | claim text | reached by verse | member word and code in that verse | status | told in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-16 | The verse puts soul and spirit beside joints and marrow, which are adjacent, and its end point is the heart: "discerning the thoughts and intentions of the heart". It shows closeness that only the word can divide. ## 6. Phrases attached to the collection (6)

A phrase is attached by the route in its link reason (a Strong's code or an English word shared with a member) and is a member of the collection: its occurrences are in sections 2 and 3 under their Strong's codes, and what the study recorded for them in sections 4 and 5. Each is shown here with its route, its occurrences and the live observation nodes whose word is the phrase.

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 23 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
